# Renovation Budgeting — Intermediate Knowledge Store

> [!NOTE]
> Initialized 2026-07-30 during the wiki/case-study reorganization. This store is a scaffold, not a completed extraction pass — no source has yet been run through the `renovation-knowledge-intake` skill's formal intake pipeline (`meeting-transcript-extract` → this store → `tiered-knowledge-base` synthesis). The sections below link to the case studies that already existed before this reorg; they are pointers, not upserted structured facts. No numeric fact in this file has been independently re-verified here — see each linked case study or archived source for provenance.

## Pending Wiki-Page Decisions

> [!NOTE]
> Added 2026-08-18, per explicit user discussion about how to track store content with no obvious wiki-page home yet, without creating a second parallel staging file (this section lives inside the store that already does that job). **Threshold rule: once a sub-topic has 3+ sources accumulated here with no existing wiki page to route them to, create the page in that session — don't defer further.** A fact that's genuinely thin/low-confidence on its own doesn't need to graduate at all; this list is only for topics with real accumulated depth and an unresolved "where does this go" question. Remove an entry once its page is created (move the note to that page's own Change Log instead) or once a source turns out to fit an existing page after all.

- **Entrance/Hallway** (1 source, added 2026-08-19): `01_Entrance/Entrance_Index.md` and `02_Hallway/Hallway_Index.md` are still project-worksheet placeholders, not accumulated-knowledge pages — the entrance-vestibule-reclamation technique from `yt_v7UXJ5fJ0H0` (moving the true entry door outward into shared-corridor/stairwell-landing space, bounded by a hard life-safety access-must-remain-open caveat) has no real home yet. Store-only for now, below the 3+ threshold.

USD-denominated, fully itemized real 52 m² Novaya Borovaya turnkey project ($70,000 total), plus a speaker-stated general 2026 Minsk market rate (~$1,000/m² typical, $700-800/m² budget floor). **Primary Minsk reference**, second data point alongside the existing Minsk World 60 m² case. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_jAdRyL6KFxs_7komnat_novaya_borovaya_52m2|extraction note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/case_studies/7komnat_novaya_borovaya_52m2_case|full case study]].
- `yt_DsdLa87Acz4` — Prolife Invest (Aristarkh Babiy) Moscow flipping-renovation cost rationale (YouTube, **manually-created captions, not ASR** — lower transcription-error risk; published 2026-07-29 [metadata-confirmed]; region = Moscow, confirmed at both spoken and metadata levels). Investor/flipping perspective, not homeowner/designer — recommendations explicitly segment-scoped (comfort/business/premium/elite) and tied to resale value, not personal preference. Secondary (Moscow/RUB, not Minsk) reference, but contributes several region-agnostic durable heuristics (independent technical supervision, stretch-ceiling flood-risk reasoning, a cheaper alternative to concealed baseboards, door-height cost economics) that nuance or extend facts already in this store. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_DsdLa87Acz4_prolife_invest_moscow_flipping|extraction note]].
- `yt_H61xa8n2nTk` — FLATART (Minsk-associated design/reno company) air-conditioning design & installation guide + retailer interview (YouTube, ASR auto-captions; published **2017-11-26 — by far the oldest source in this store**; region Minsk-associated at level 2 only (title/tags), not spoken in the transcript itself; currency ambiguous, "рублей" plausibly BYN by inference from company location, not confirmed). All pricing treated as historical-only given the 2017 date. Contributes the store's **first in-depth AC/climate-system technical content**: 3 indoor-unit placement rules, a dry-trap condensate-drainage mechanism explanation, an inverter-vs-standard explanation, a rough sizing rule of thumb (~1 kW/10 m², +20% buffer), and a top-3 buyer/installer mistakes list. Several numbers in this source are ASR-garbled or internally inconsistent (see extraction note) and were not guessed at. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_H61xa8n2nTk_flatart_ac_installation_guide|extraction note]].
- `yt__hYkf4UkCqw` — "Дома Минска" documented renovation series, Episode 1 (YouTube; published 2025-12-11 [metadata-confirmed]; region Minsk/Belarus confirmed at level 1 — **spoken directly and repeatedly, the strongest regional evidence of any source in this store**). Episode 1 of an ongoing series following one owner's bare-shell 49 m² apartment (ЖК "Северный берег") from start to finish — **contains no completed renovation budget**, only a property purchase price (79,000 EUR / "$1,860/m²" — two different currencies stated for the same purchase with no conversion basis given, not reconciled) and undecided/leaning design choices. Contributes genuinely new **Minsk-specific replanning/approval regulatory detail** (исполком approval for wet-zone relocation, plumbing riser capping when merging bathrooms, a height threshold for wall-hung fixture approval). No case study created — no cost data exists yet to build one around; flagged as a series worth revisiting if later episodes are processed. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT__hYkf4UkCqw_doma_minska_severny_bereg_ep1|extraction note]].
- `yt_QHl1YEHMfgE` — "Дома Минска" series, Episode 2 (YouTube; published 2026-04-07 [metadata-confirmed]; same project/apartment as `yt__hYkf4UkCqw`, but region **not independently re-confirmed at level 1 in this episode's own speech** — inherited from series continuity only). A professional designer (Elena Belitskaya) walks through layout/planning principles against the real floor plan — **no pricing content at all**. Contributes the store's richest set of universal, brand-agnostic technical/planning facts to date (room-count-vs-window-count, drainage-slope-aware kitchen relocation, bed-clearance ergonomics, door-leaf-width standards, toilet-first bathroom sequencing, and a critical "zashivka vs. venshakhta" distinction — the latter genuinely important and not present in any prior source). Also contains a promotional element (the host's direct personal endorsement of the designer) flagged separately, per this store's advertising filter, without discrediting her technical claims. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_QHl1YEHMfgE_doma_minska_severny_bereg_ep2_layout|extraction note]].
- `yt_pC7CO-rpwBs` — "Команда Мастеров" turnkey studio pitch/cost-breakdown video (YouTube; published 2024-10-01, second-oldest source in this store; region = Belarus confirmed at level 1 but **country-level only, not city-level**; Minsk association is level 2 only via Instagram handle). **Turnkey/Full-Service** by design — a 7-stage $/m² budget framework (design, labor, rough materials, finish materials, appliances, fixtures, custom furniture). **Arithmetic cross-check performed (level 4)**: the stated overall range "$1,100 to $18,800/m²" — the low end matches summing the 7 stages' own minimums almost exactly ($1,099), but the high end does not match summing the maximums ($1,749) at all; "$18,800" is treated as an ASR/speech error and **not used** — the arithmetic-reconstructed $1,749 is recorded instead, with reasoning shown. A third Belarus turnkey data point, roughly consistent with (if somewhat above) 7komnat.by's "~$1,000/m² typical" market claim. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_pC7CO-rpwBs_komanda_masterov_7stage_budget|extraction note]].
- `yt_cdNwbqsLUK4` — "РемонтХочу" (remonthochu.ru) smeta-literacy/self-calculation guide (YouTube; published 2024-11-14; region = Moscow **and Moscow Oblast**, named directly and specifically in speech — more precise than most Moscow sources in this store). **Turnkey company's own construction-only smeta** — 11 stages, rough+finish labor (materials likely bundled per line item, `inferred` not confirmed), explicitly **excludes design fee and furniture/appliances** (unlike this store's broader turnkey totals) — do not compare its $/m² directly against those without adjusting scope. **Two arithmetic cross-checks performed (level 4)**: (1) an ASR-garbled per-m² plaster rate ("от полу тысяч") resolved to exactly 1,500 RUB/m² by dividing the stage's own stated total by its own stated area; (2) a missing flooring subtotal (never stated aloud) recovered exactly (127,500 RUB = 85 m² × 1,500 RUB/m²) by reconciling the grand total against the sum of all other explicitly-stated stages — a clean, non-guessed recovery. Contributes the store's richest set of **smeta-literacy/anti-upsell heuristics** (price by fixture points not pipe length, price electrical by floor area not cable length, demand a fully-itemized putty/skim-coat scope, verify flooring pattern is specified, watch for common-area covering requirements and mid-project scope additions) — durable and delivery-model-agnostic, a strong match for this project's stated preference for universal knowledge. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_cdNwbqsLUK4_remonthochu_smeta_methodology|extraction note]].
- `yt_OrKB6uSKRyk` — "Бородатый Прораб" (Vladimir Vorobyov) Moscow business-class renovation cost rationale (YouTube; published 2025-01-08; region = Moscow, confirmed at level 1, city named directly and repeatedly). **Turnkey/Full-Service**, self-promotional own-company channel — the **broadest single figure in this store to date**: "from 150,000 RUB/m²" for a full, move-in-ready realization (design + labor + rough/finish materials + furniture + specialized installation). Three-tier design-project pricing structure (4,000/5,000/7,000 RUB/m²) is genuinely useful methodology. Two ASR issues: a large-format-tile rate rendered as "88,000 RUB/m²" is almost certainly a digit-duplication error for ~8,000 (contextual "×2" cue), recorded as `uncertain`; a rough-materials base rate has a **missing leading digit** in the transcript and was **not reconstructed at all** (left as a genuine gap). Contributes a nuance to the guide's existing flat "labor ≈30-35% of total" assumption (this source's own labor share drops to ~20% at premium tiers) and corroborates, from an independent practitioner, `yt_DsdLa87Acz4`'s theme that personalized high-end renovations don't resell at cost. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_OrKB6uSKRyk_borodaty_prorab_moscow_business_class|extraction note]].
- `yt_8zm_5NjbGNA` — Яна Врублевская personal renovation vlog, "Ремонт за 10000$. Цены на все. Минск Мир" (YouTube; published 2023-05-01 [metadata-confirmed], **second-oldest source in this store after FLATART's 2017**; region "Минск Мир" named in title/tags/location-metadata, but **zero spoken mentions anywhere in the transcript** — level 2 only, checked directly). **Personal channel, not a company/contractor channel** — the apartment owner narrating her own completed self-managed studio renovation, a different promotional profile than most sources here. **Self-Managed/Itemized, the fullest end-to-end self-managed real project in this store** — covers both rough works (labor+materials, BYN) and the full finish/fixture/appliance/furniture shopping list, unlike the store's previous closest comparable (labor-only 44 m² смета). Notably transparent about exchange-rate drift affecting her own project (BYN/USD moved 2.6→2.8 during the renovation). **Level-4 arithmetic cross-check performed**: itemized rough-works line items sum to 10,665 BYN against her stated 11,250 BYN total (585 BYN gap, plausibly an unenumerated item per her own caveat, not resolved). Real-world outcome: **~30% budget overshoot** against her own stated $10,000 target (final ≈$12,957). Contributes several soft-cost line items (new-build handover inspection, temporary toilet on day one, per-riser shutoff fee, developer registration fee) not previously recorded in this store. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_8zm_5NjbGNA_yana_vrublevskaya_minsk_mir_10k_studio|extraction note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/case_studies/yana_vrublevskaya_minsk_mir_studio_2023_case|full case study]].

- `yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw` — BURO / Artem Oganyan design-studio segment-tier pricing deep-dive (YouTube; published 2026-03-12; region = St. Petersburg, **level 2 only** — the transcript's single "Петербург" mention is about furniture-manufacturing origin, not the studio's location, checked directly and not conflated). **Turnkey/Full-Service, the highest price tier of any source in this store** — mid/premium/luxury segment ranges of 200,000–450,000 / 450,000–1,000,000 / 1,000,000+ RUB/m², even the mid floor exceeding Бородатый Прораб's previous "broadest" 150,000 RUB/m² figure. Contributes the store's strongest brand-agnostic conceptual framing to date: "$/m² without context is meaningless" (2–10× variance for the same visual result), quality-vs-durability as distinct concepts, "wishes vs. expectations" as a cost driver independent of segment, and a nuance-accumulation example (laminate vs. engineered wood) extending the existing vague-smeta-entry theme. **Third independent source now corroborating the resale/personalization-mismatch theme** (alongside `yt_DsdLa87Acz4` and `yt_OrKB6uSKRyk`). A concrete worked example shows AC/ventilation choice alone spanning ~5,000–50,000 RUB/m² for the same 100 m² apartment — folded into `12_Engineering_and_Systems/HVAC_and_Ventilation.md`. Full brand-by-category specification list (self-promotional, this studio's own choices) recorded in a companion case study, not imported into the guide. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_6Z7uH2_rXsw_buro_segment_pricing_2026|extraction note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/case_studies/buro_segment_pricing_2026_case|full case study]].

Routed to Electrical/Lighting, HVAC, Wardrobes/Storage, Doors/Trim, Walls/Paint, Budgeting/Planning. [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_K-W4dLJGEtg_best_solutions_6_194|note]].

Also genuinely new: entrance-door ergonomic-clearance standard, mirror-on-door fallback rule (<40cm wall space), ceiling-box-to-furniture cross-trade tolerance lesson (moving to a flush-furniture + top-module approach), wardrobe-integrated-server phase-out rationale, recessed-radiator "free floor space" spatial argument, mandatory kitchen-living AC planning rule with retrofit-cost rationale, switch-to-fixture nearest-switch mapping rule (company-tested), split galley-kitchen layout technique, flush bathroom-floor waterproofing/soundproofing standard, and two new price points (concealed hinge ~3,000 vs. standard ~700 RUB; concealed fixture set ~280,000 vs. standard ~70,000 RUB).   - `yt_DaN3r4wgpc` — "335K Down the Drain, Thanks to the Designer #248" (2023-10-31) — real figure (335,000 RUB, zero refunded). New: whole-apartment task-location-check technique (a general planning discipline extending the existing bathroom-specific "walk through the use scenario" rule), duct-box protrusion-measurement corroboration, kitchen-living passage-width data point (900mm), existing-wall radiator-recessing technique. [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_DaN3r4wgpc_335k_thanks_designer_248|note]].
  - `yt_pjqvWz_5c9Q` — "Designer Disaster for 7.5 Million #247" (2023-10-29) — **PARTIAL, thin**: fourth instance of the "as the buyer" format, near-total overlap with this same sub-series' other videos. Only 2 new points: coat-hook-shoulder-height rule, over-zoning-flooring-materials caution. [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_pjqvWz_5c9Q_designer_disaster_7.5m_247|note]].
  - `yt_M4dxo-6kcDs` — "Total Disaster for 540K, Thanks to the Designer #242" (2023-10-10) — dense with genuinely new techniques: the "truncated closets" pattern (named, deliberate design technique), split-loggia-into-two-zones technique, a furniture-fabrication tolerance figure (4-5cm minimum wedge width), a kitchen-door-swing-clearance distinction, and an actionable designer-vetting heuristic ("demand dimensional justification before approving aesthetics"). Sixth corroborating instance of the 150mm partition figure (used 3 times in this video alone). [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_M4dxo-6kcDs_540k_thanks_designer_242|note]].
  - `yt_2E7YVK6PhIM` — "638K to the Landfill, Thanks to the Designer #241" (2023-10-03) — **the standout of this chunk**: two genuinely excellent, previously-unrecorded techniques — a glass-sliding-partition workaround for gas-kitchen code compliance in an open-plan layout, and a "future-flexible" pre-wired partition-wall technique (engineer a future wall's anchor points into the current build now, so a later life-stage change can wall off a room cheaply). Three real comparative design-fee data points (320k/50m², 420k/78.5m², 638k/145m², same unnamed designer). [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_2E7YVK6PhIM_638k_thanks_designer_241|note]].
  - `yt_e1HRByaCFv0` — "Designer Disaster for 420 Thousand #239" (2023-09-26) — confirmed as "the second object" from the same designer as `yt_2E7YVK6PhIM`. PARTIAL: companion future-flexible-wiring technique (a hidden electrical point behind wallpaper for a future single-to-double-bed conversion), a gaming-screen-angle mechanism distinct from general TV-distance content, and a real fee-recovery data point (420k paid, 220k recovered through direct pressure). [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_e1HRByaCFv0_420k_designer_disaster_239|note]].

  **Chunk 4 retrospective — closes out Category 5 in its entirety.** The final chunk skewed toward planning-technique richness over raw defect-cataloguing: three "future-flexible planning" techniques emerged independently across three different videos (a gas-code-compliant glass partition, a pre-wired future partition wall, and pre-wired future electrical for a furniture change) — different specific applications of the same underlying principle (engineer a known future need's rough-in now, concealed behind replaceable finish, rather than deferring it to a later renovation) — genuinely one of the more broadly useful planning philosophies to emerge from this entire Category 5 pass. The 150mm-soundproofing-partition figure reached its sixth corroborating instance. Two videos in the "as the buyer" sarcastic sub-series showed heavy diminishing returns (both largely corroborative, one thin) — worth noting for any future similar-format batch that this specific sub-series' marginal value drops sharply after the first 1-2 entries once its core findings are already captured. **Wiki-routing performed the same session, not deferred**: `13_Surfaces_and_Finishes/Walls_and_Paint.md` (new "Future-Flexible Planning & Regulatory Workarounds" section covering all three chunk-4 standout techniques, 150mm-partition update to 6 sources). **`Budgeting_Guide.md` not updated** — financial figures remain thin/single-account throughout. CSV rows to be logged (`run_20260819_lagL0yz7J28` through `run_20260819_e1HRByaCFv0`, all `archived`). Promotion self-check performed for all 6 source notes.

  **Category 5 full-category retrospective (all 4 further-pool chunks + 2 earlier trials, 37 videos total processed across 2026-08-17 through 2026-08-19)**: this category — initially found "thin yield" in a 2026-08-05 baseline trial — was ultimately the most productive tier of the full-channel triage once given a real multi-chunk trial. Standout cross-chunk findings: the 150mm-soundproofing-partition figure (6 independent corroborating instances), the bathroom-floor-level leak-containment mechanism (3 corroborating instances), the "future-flexible planning" technique family (3 independent applications in the final chunk alone), the opportunity-cost-of-DIY-design pattern (3 corroborating instances in chunk 2), and a genuine consumer-fraud finding (fabricated underfloor heating, 2nd trial). Two format variants worth remembering for any future similar batch: the first-person "as the buyer" sarcastic critique sub-series (strong initially, thin by its 3rd-4th entry) and the "unnamed repeat-offender designer" mini-series (real, useful comparative fee data, but no legal-dispute content since no designer is ever named by this channel). **This closes out Category 5 and the entire original full-channel triage** — see the triage file's own final retrospective for the complete channel-level summary.

## Cross-Source Comparison Tables

Per explicit user guidance: forcing all sources into one grand $/m² total
doesn't work well, since different sources measure different scopes
(labor-only vs. materials-bundled, construction-only vs. full-realization
with furniture). Instead, this section groups **comparable pieces of
information by topic** — the same stage, trade, or material category
across sources — so real overlaps and gaps are visible without pretending
incompatible totals are the same thing. Nothing here is averaged or
force-converted between currencies; figures are shown side by side with
their own units, and the reader draws the comparison.

## Advertising / Promotional Content Notes

Per explicit user guidance: renovation-content channels are very often
run by the company doing the work, a sponsored partner, or a
real-estate/supplier affiliate — expected to be the **majority** of
sources processed for this topic (the user estimates 70-80% of sources
provided are from turnkey/full-service companies). This section flags
each source's own promotional format and which specific content within
it should be read as a commercial mention rather than a neutral,
universal recommendation. **Nothing below is deleted from the sections
that follow** — brand/property mentions stay in the record with their
sourcing, but should not be treated as validated general advice, and
none of them were carried into `Budgeting_Guide.md` (which stays brand-
and property-name-free by design — confirmed clean on review).

**A finer distinction, per follow-up user guidance**: within a
turnkey-company source, separate (a) **general market-rate/technique
information** the company reports (usually reasonably fair, since it
describes a real market the company also operates in) from (b) **the
specific tier/product/service the company steers the viewer toward**
(carries a structural incentive toward the company's own higher margin,
even when phrased as neutral advice — a turnkey company's revenue scales
with the tier a client picks). When a source's brand/tier mentions below
are tagged "commercial mention," this is the distinction being applied:
the surrounding market data is treated as normal evidence, but the
specific recommended choice is not adopted as a default without noting
that a cheaper in-category alternative likely exists in the same market
for a self-managed buyer.

- **`dfc91747`/`26bc9c25` (Minsk World 60 m² case)** — **checked retroactively (2026-07-31), previously unflagged.** The presenter opens with "тему, которая беспокоит буквально каждого **нашего** клиента" ("a topic that worries every one of *our* clients") — this is the renovation company presenting its own completed client project as a showcase, the same self-case-study format as `yt_jAdRyL6KFxs`. Self-promotional by format. The itemized costs themselves remain treated as reliable (arithmetic-consistent, already the guide's primary Minsk benchmark), but the project should be read as a marketing showcase, not an independent third-party audit.
- **`b385361e` (44 m² Minsk смета case)** — **checked retroactively (2026-07-31), previously unflagged.** The presenter identifies himself as a designer/former all-trades builder who now works only on design projects, and closes with an explicit lead-generation CTA ("если вам нужен дизайн-проект... оставляйте заявку на сайте либо пишите мне в Telegram/WhatsApp/Viber"). Self-promotional by format. Notably, the presenter is candid that his own smeta prices are "average market rate," explicitly warns that going cheaper is possible but risky, and recommends hiring a foreman ("прораб") for oversight — mild tier-steering-adjacent framing (recommending an added paid role), but delivered with stated reasoning (a personal anecdote about a client who skipped wall-leveling and later disputed quality with a hired assessor) rather than a bare recommendation. The labor-only rate card itself remains this store's closest self-managed comparable dataset.
- **`yt_BMmPLHVmnqw`** — the channel is the speaker's own business
  (WITALT). Self-promotional format (own channel, own services). Brand
  mentions affected: Tikkurila/Dulux paint, Kerama Marazzi/ATC Concord
  tile, Far fittings, ATC electrical fixtures, Gauss bulbs — all the
  speaker's personal opinion on his own channel, already tagged
  `unverified` in this store, now additionally flagged as
  self-promotional context. The description's "capsule solution" side
  offer (already excluded from this store, see Risks/Gaps) is a direct
  product plug. **Retrofitted market-data-vs-tier-steering split (2026-07-31)**:
  the general price-tier framework (100–120 тыс./м², the labor-tier
  scale) is market-rate context, kept as usable evidence; the specific
  brand picks (Tikkurila/Dulux, Kerama Marazzi, Far, ATC, Gauss) are this
  speaker's own tier choices on his own channel, not neutral market fact
  — a self-managed buyer sourcing the same categories should expect
  cheaper in-category alternatives to exist.
- **`yt_jAdRyL6KFxs`** — this is a company (7komnat.by) showcasing its
  own director's own apartment as a case study: **inherently
  self-promotional in format**, even though the cost figures themselves
  were arithmetic-cross-checked and are treated as reliable data. The
  video description additionally lists named suppliers (keramika.by,
  xfloor.by, santake.by, Virtoni.by, Devi.by, art-teplo.by, Litokol,
  samovolka.by) as a promotional partner/supplier list — these were
  never adopted as store recommendations and are not repeated here as
  advice.
- **`yt_DsdLa87Acz4`** — the speaker explicitly promotes his own agency
  (Prolife Invest) at the end of the video: self-promotional format.
  Brand mentions affected: Blum hardware, Bosch/Asko/Miele
  appliance-tier recommendations — treated as the speaker's professional
  opinion, not verified/neutral fact, consistent with existing
  `unverified` tags. (Note: several of his brand *criticisms*, e.g.
  arguing against overpaying for Omoikiri faucets, are the opposite of
  promotional — skepticism toward a named brand doesn't carry the same
  advertising risk as a recommendation toward one.) **Retrofitted
  market-data-vs-tier-steering split (2026-07-31)**: the segment-tier
  framework itself (comfort/business/premium/elite) and the brigade
  labor-rate figures are market-rate context, kept as usable evidence;
  the specific brand picks within each segment (Bosch for business-class,
  Asko for premium, Miele for elite, Blum hardware) are this speaker's
  own tier recommendations, not neutral fact — though his skepticism
  toward Omoikiri is the opposite case and isn't subject to this caveat.
- **`yt_H61xa8n2nTk`** — **highest advertising-format risk in this
  store**: this is a joint interview between the host's own company
  (FLATART) and a named business partner store ("Климат24"), i.e. the
  brand-tier recommendations (Electrolux Monaco, Daikin, Mitsubishi
  Electric) come directly from a retailer being interviewed by his own
  business associate about products he sells. The neutral technical
  content (placement rules, dry-trap drainage mechanism, sizing rule of
  thumb) is not brand-dependent and isn't affected by this — but the
  specific brand-tier value judgments and warranty claims should be read
  as a sales pitch, not independent product testing.
- **`yt__hYkf4UkCqw`** — the channel is monetized via a sponsor-gift
  call-out ("подарок от наших партнёров" — a prize from "our partners"
  for guessing the renovation cost) and an embedded real-estate-agent
  recruitment ad in the description; the video also showcases one
  specific residential complex (ЖК "Северный берег") somewhat favorably
  (host calls it "the most talked-about complex of 2025" and frames the
  owner's above-market purchase price as justified). The Minsk
  regulatory facts extracted from this source (исполком approval,
  riser-capping) are not brand/property-dependent and aren't affected by
  this flag, but the property itself should not be read as a neutral
  recommendation.
- **`yt_QHl1YEHMfgE`** — same channel/promotional format as
  `yt__hYkf4UkCqw` (inherited: sponsor gift, recruitment ad, property
  showcase), **plus a new element**: the host personally endorses the
  guest designer by name at the close of the video and links her
  Instagram in the description — a referral/endorsement of a specific
  professional, not neutral peer review. The designer's *technical*
  claims (ergonomics, drainage constraints, the zashivka/venshakhta
  distinction) are evaluated on their own engineering merit and are not
  discredited by the endorsement — but the endorsement itself is
  promotional content, not evidence of her expertise.
- **`yt_pC7CO-rpwBs`** — the host explicitly identifies the channel as
  the studio's own ("Команда Мастеров"), with repeated lead-generation
  CTAs (intake form, Telegram/WhatsApp manager contact) — classic
  self-promotional turnkey-studio format. Applying the market-data-vs-
  tier-steering distinction: the 7-stage $/m² price ranges are treated as
  usable general market data; the studio's own material defaults (Egger
  particleboard + Blum hardware as their base furniture spec, avoiding a
  named premium lighting retailer "Квазар") are the studio's own tier
  choices, not neutral market fact.
- **`yt_cdNwbqsLUK4`** — the host is the founder of "РемонтХочу," with a
  mid-video CTA for the company's own "прозрачные сметы" service in
  Moscow/Moscow Oblast and an end CTA to submit a request. Self-
  promotional per format, but the content itself is unusually
  consumer-protective (teaches viewers to catch *other* companies'
  upsell tricks) — good marketing for this company, but also genuinely
  reusable methodology independent of who a viewer hires. The specific

## Renovation Delivery Model Classification

Per explicit user guidance: this project's own plan is **self-managed /
piecemeal** (hiring individual specialists per trade, sourcing materials
directly), not **turnkey / full-service** (one company bundles design,
management, and construction into one priced package). These produce
genuinely different $/m² figures for comparable underlying work — a
turnkey price includes management/design/coordination overhead a
self-managed budget doesn't pay for. Every cost-bearing source/case in
this store is classified below so figures are never blended across
delivery models.

- **Minsk World 60 m² case** (`dfc91747`/`26bc9c25`, $54,000 / $900/m²) —
  **Turnkey / Full-Service**. Explicitly a "Turnkey Designer Apartment"
  project: custom millwork, furniture, electronics, and full finishing
  bundled into one project total.
- **44 m² Minsk labor смета** (`b385361e`, see
  [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/case_studies/price_table_screenshot_case|Price Table Screenshot Case]]) —
  **Self-Managed / Itemized, Labor-Only**. The presenter explicitly
  describes the `Цена` column as **average market rate** (not
  premium, not cheapest) for individually-priced work items, materials
  tracked separately, no company-management overhead bundled in. **This
  is the closest comparable dataset in this store to this project's own
  planned approach** — see the rate-card table already in that case
  study ("Companion Benchmark" section) for concrete per-item USD/BYN
  rates.
- **7komnat.by Novaya Borovaya 52 m² case** (`yt_jAdRyL6KFxs`, $70,000 /
  $1,346/m²) — **Turnkey / Full-Service**. The company's own case study
  of its own director's apartment; the four budget groups explicitly
  bundle design, approvals, all labor, and all furniture/appliances into
  one company-managed total.
- **`yt_BMmPLHVmnqw` (WITALT)** — **Mixed**. The 100–120 тыс. руб./м²
  headline figure describes the speaker's own full-service company's
  typical client budget tier (Turnkey-leaning), but the same source
  separately discusses "бригада" (crew) labor-only pricing tiers
  (20/30/40/60/80 тыс. руб./м²) that are closer to a Self-Managed/
  Labor-Only framing depending on which tier a reader hires directly vs.
  through a managed company. Not cleanly one category — read the
  specific figure's own context before citing it.
- **`yt_DsdLa87Acz4` (Prolife Invest)** — **Labor-Only, self-managed-
  leaning**. The 30-35-40 тыс. руб./м² "brigade" rate is explicitly
  framed by the speaker as what you pay hiring a multi-trade crew
  directly, contrasted with 50-100 тыс. руб./м² "market" pricing for the
  same scope (implicitly including more managed/institutional
  positioning). The speaker's own agency does flipping-focused renovation
  management, but this specific figure describes direct-hire crew
  pricing, not the agency's own bundled service price.
- **`yt_H61xa8n2nTk` (FLATART)** — **Itemized / Service-Specific**, not a
  whole-project figure at all — a per-linear-meter AC installation labor
  rate, historical (2017) regardless.
- **`yt__hYkf4UkCqw` / `yt_QHl1YEHMfgE` (Дома Минска, Severny Bereg
  series)** — **Not yet classifiable**, no completed cost data exists in
  either episode processed so far. Worth noting: the production appears
  to be assembling **individual specialists** (a separate designer, a
  separate replanning-approval specialist) rather than working through
  one turnkey company, which — *if* later episodes confirm this pattern
  — could make this series a genuinely useful real-world example of the
  self-managed approach. Flagged as worth watching, not asserted yet.
- **`yt_pC7CO-rpwBs` (Команда Мастеров)** — **Turnkey / Full-Service**.
  Explicitly bundles design, all labor (incl. foreman/project
  management), materials, appliances, fixtures, and custom furniture into
  one 7-stage pitch. A third Belarus turnkey data point, not a
  self-managed reference.
- **`yt_cdNwbqsLUK4` (РемонтХочу)** — **Turnkey company's own smeta,
  narrower scope: construction only.** Rough+finish labor (likely
  materials-bundled, `inferred`), but explicitly **excludes** design fee
  and furniture/appliances — a different scope than this store's other
  "full turnkey" figures. Don't compare its $/m² directly against those
  without scope-adjusting first.
- **`yt_OrKB6uSKRyk` (Бородатый Прораб)** — **Turnkey / Full-Service, the
  broadest scope in this store to date.** The 150,000 RUB/m² "full
  realization" figure explicitly bundles design, all labor, rough +
  finish materials, furniture, and specialized installation (doors, AC,
  smart home, ventilation, stretch ceilings) into one move-in-ready
  total — a wider scope than any other turnkey figure in this store.
- **`yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw` (BURO)** — **Turnkey / Full-Service, now the
  broadest scope AND highest price tier in this store, superseding
  Бородатый Прораб.** Explicit "under key" realization across three
  segments (200,000–450,000 / 450,000–1,000,000 / 1,000,000+ RUB/m²).
  Not a self-managed reference at all — useful for calibration (what a
  given quality tier actually buys), not as a target figure for this
  project's own plan.
- **`yt_8zm_5NjbGNA` (Яна Врублевская, Minsk Mir studio, 2023)** —
  **Self-Managed / Itemized, the fullest end-to-end self-managed real
  project in this store to date.** Individual specialists hired per
  trade, all materials/fixtures/appliances/furniture sourced
  independently — no managing company, no bundled design fee anywhere in
  the narration. Unlike the 44 m² смета (labor-only), this source covers
  the **complete** self-managed cost picture: rough works AND finish
  materials/fixtures/appliances/furniture for one real project. **The
  single closest structural match to this project's own planned approach
  found so far** — see full comparison in the case study §6.
- **`yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU` (Zemstandart, kids-room mistakes/tips, #181)** —
  **Labor-Only**, restating the company's own comfort-class (25,000–
  45,000 RUB/m²) vs. business-class (50,000–85,000 RUB/m²) rate bands
  already recorded from `yt_7VfZIYGUrTo` — same speaker (Sergey Saratov),
  two days apart, treated as one standing rate card, not two data points.
  The rest of the source (technique/mistakes content) carries no
  pricing.
- **`yt_OCjVd5HOcac` (Zemstandart/zemspro, video #528)** — **Turnkey /
  Full-Service.** The free "zems design" offer is explicitly bundled
  with, and only obtainable through, ordering a full project with the
  company. The 331,000 RUB/m² (107,000 labor + 224,000 materials)
  average and the ~1,000,000 RUB/74 m² bespoke design fee both describe
  this company's own bespoke-design-tier client population, self-reported
  by the designer — not a self-managed or market-wide figure. No
  self-managed/itemized data in this source.

## Durable Facts

- A complete interior design project package should include: a dimensioned as-built plan, a demolition plan, and a clean (unfurnished) montage/installation plan; a full set of wall elevations cross-checked against the plans; visualizations used for client sign-off; and node/section drawings resolving junction details. Detailed MEP (electrical/HVAC) engineering schematics are typically a *separate* deliverable from a standard interior design project, not included by default. `confirmed` [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- The visible price gap between a surface-mounted and a built-in (behind-the-wall) plumbing fixture is commonly much smaller than the real installed-cost gap once pipework, fittings, sleeves, and labor are counted — comparing device price alone understates built-in installs. `confirmed` as the source's claim; the specific RUB multiplier behind it is `unverified` (single source). [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- A renovation budget should be planned from the client's actual, reliable available funds (excluding loans, credit lines, and not-yet-earned future income) rather than an aspirational total figure; not clarifying this up front is reported as a common cause of mid-project shortfalls and client/designer disputes. `confirmed` [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- Higher-priced contractor service tiers (on this source's own pricing scale) are described as buying more project-management staffing and process (foreman, senior master, procurement coordinator, supervising designer), not necessarily higher-skilled physical labor. `confirmed` (stated as the speaker's own view/opinion, not verified independently). [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- The channel/video is associated with "Юрий Петров, руководитель WITALT" (Yuri Petrov, head of WITALT) per the video description's sign-off. `confirmed` at level 2 (metadata) that this is the channel owner's stated identity; `inferred` (level 3) that he is the actual on-camera speaker, since the ASR transcript has no diarization/name tags to confirm this directly. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- `yt_jAdRyL6KFxs` is a 7komnat.by company case study: Vladimir Ermakov (founder) interviews Aleksey (company director) about Aleksey's own just-completed apartment. Both roles are stated directly in the transcript's opening lines. `confirmed` (level 1) — stronger speaker attribution than most sources processed for this topic, though note this is an internal/marketing case study (the client is the company's own director), not an independent third party. [source: yt_jAdRyL6KFxs]
- **Usable room count is constrained by window count** (a balcony door counts as a quasi-window) before client preference is even considered — more rooms than usable light sources tends to produce impractically small, poorly-lit rooms; a combined "euro-format" layout (one bedroom + one large kitchen-living room) is preferable to splitting a modest apartment into three small rooms. `confirmed`, general design principle. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- **Relocating a kitchen/sink must account for drainage slope, not just supply-line routing**: pressurized water-supply lines can be hidden in screed with little constraint, but gravity-fed sewage lines need a consistent slope toward the riser — the farther a sink is from the riser, the more screed depth is needed to conceal the sloped pipe; placing a sink along a wall (drain routed inside the wall to the riser) avoids this problem better than a mid-room placement. `confirmed`, non-obvious and genuinely useful. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- **Toilet placement should drive bathroom layout, not the reverse**: place the toilet first, as close to the riser as practical, because it requires a 100 mm-diameter drain pipe — the hardest pipe size to conceal — then plan the sink/shower positions around it. `confirmed`, a concrete sequencing rule. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- **"Zashivka" (plumbing cladding box) vs. "venshakhta" (ventilation shaft) is a critical, non-obvious distinction**: a zashivka (developer-built cladding around risers, sometimes oversized) can be demolished and rebuilt more compactly to reclaim space; a venshakhta (ventilation shaft, identifiable by grille openings, typically ~60×40 cm) runs as a single shared vertical column from ground floor to roof serving every apartment on that riser line and **must never be touched, damaged, or removed** — doing so cuts ventilation for every unit sharing the column, not just the renovating one. `confirmed` — the single most important safety-relevant technical fact in this store to date. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- A design project deliverable in a real Minsk turnkey project included 3D visualization and covered replanning approval (moving partitions/radiators, a bathroom doorway shift, a waterproofing sub-project, and obtaining a new technical passport via BRTI) as one bundled service. `confirmed` (level 1). [source: yt_jAdRyL6KFxs]
- A stretch ceiling can contain/retain water from a leak above (forming a temporary pocket) before it's drained or punctured, limiting damage compared to a drywall (GKL) ceiling, which saturates and lets water through to furniture/floors/walls below. `single-account`, anecdotal (one specific flood example given), but it's a distinct risk-based argument for stretch ceilings beyond cost. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- Recommendations for appliance/finish "prestige" level are commonly scoped to a property's market segment (e.g. comfort/business/premium/elite) by resale-focused practitioners, rather than applied as a blanket "always buy cheap" or "always buy premium" rule. `confirmed` as the speaker's stated framework; whether buyers actually perceive/value this distinction is not independently verified. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- A cheap, thin "micro-baseboard" profile exists as a lower-cost visual approximation of a fully concealed in-wall baseboard, trading a small amount of visual/tactile fidelity for a large reduction in installation cost and time (speaker estimates roughly half a day vs. about a month of added schedule for a 100 m² apartment). `confirmed` as the speaker's stated claim; not independently verified. This directly nuances the existing store fact (from `yt_BMmPLHVmnqw`) praising fully concealed baseboards without mentioning this alternative. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- Indoor split-system AC units should follow three placement rules: don't blow cold air directly on occupants (cool the room evenly instead); leave clear space above and below the unit for airflow; position it to be visually unobtrusive. `confirmed`. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- Condensate from an AC indoor unit should not drain straight into a sewer stack, and a simple water trap is inadequate since the trap water eventually evaporates during idle periods, losing its seal against sewer-gas backflow. The correct mechanism is a "dry trap" valve using a floating ball that blocks the drain opening once water evaporates. `confirmed` — a genuinely new technical mechanism, not present in any prior source in this store. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- Labor as a percentage of a renovation's total cost is tier-dependent, not fixed — one Moscow company's own labor rate is ~30-40% of a comfort-tier total but only ~20% of a premium-tier total for the same company, because furniture/finish spend grows faster than labor scope at higher tiers. `single-account`, nuances the guide's existing flat "labor ≈30-35% of total" figure. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- A highly personalized, high-quality renovation typically will not be valued at its full cost by the resale market, which tends to credit renovations closer to a generic baseline regardless of actual build quality, because individualized taste doesn't universally appeal to buyers — cited as the reason flipping-focused renovations deliberately use cheaper, more generic finishes. `single-account`, but corroborates the same theme independently raised by a different practitioner (`yt_DsdLa87Acz4`) already in this store. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- Skipping a flush pre-filter to save money can cause sediment to jam a wall-hung toilet's cistern float mechanism, especially after water is shut off for an extended period (e.g. vacation) — a specific, concrete failure mode, not a vague caution. `confirmed`, real incident cited by the source. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- AC installation quality (not equipment quality) is described by a climate-equipment retailer as roughly 80% of what determines reliability and lifespan; specialized tools (vacuum pumps, flaring tools, manometers) and training are required, making DIY installation from online tutorials alone inadvisable. `single-account`, and the speaker sells installation services (potential self-interest bias, noted). [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- In Minsk, relocating a "wet point" (e.g. moving a kitchen to a different room) requires a technical replanning project and approval from the local исполком (executive committee) and other authorities before work can proceed. `confirmed` as the host's own stated practical experience with prior replanning projects; not sourced to a named regulation. [source: yt__hYkf4UkCqw]
- Merging two separate bathrooms (each on its own plumbing riser) typically means one riser is capped while the other remains in use; there is a specific height threshold above which a suspended/wall-hung fixture (e.g. a wall-hung toilet in a false wall) cannot be installed/approved without going through the replanning approval process. `confirmed`, same source/basis as above. This is the store's first Minsk-specific regulatory/approval detail. [source: yt__hYkf4UkCqw]
- A property's per-m² purchase price is a distinct category from its renovation cost per m² and must not be blended with renovation-cost figures elsewhere in this store. [source: yt__hYkf4UkCqw]
- A proper putty/skim-coat job for paint-ready walls has up to ~9-10 distinct sub-steps (primer, base putty, sanding, priming, fiberglass mesh, priming, finish putty, sanding, priming/paint); a smeta line that just reads "wall putty" with no sub-step breakdown makes it impossible to verify what was actually done or catch a missing step afterward. `confirmed`, a concrete transparency/verification rule. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- A ~26% contingency buffer (roughly 1,000,000 RUB on a ~3.87M RUB base) was added explicitly by a professional company to its own smeta for "indirect/unaccounted work" — notably higher than this project's guide's existing stated 10–15% buffer figure. `single-account`, not adopted as a replacement figure, recorded as a comparison data point. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- **"Price per m² without context is close to meaningless"** — the same visual result can vary 2–5×, sometimes up to 10×, in real cost depending on material/build quality, decision complexity, and specification level. `confirmed`, stated directly and illustrated with multiple concrete worked examples (tile quality vs. durability, engineering system choice, flooring nuances). One of the most broadly useful framing statements found in any source for this topic. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- **Quality and durability are related but distinct concepts for a material**: e.g. budget and expensive porcelain tile in a wet zone can be equally durable (both waterproof, functionally fine long-term), but differ in quality (geometry, flatness, dimensional stability, print precision) — and quality determines whether the tile can even be laid flat and even, independent of installer skill. `confirmed`. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- **"Wishes" (a specific named brand/product target) and "expectations" (a described functional requirement without a named brand) are cost drivers independent of market segment** — both can produce up to ~10× cost variance on a single line item (e.g. a budget vs. a specifically-named premium oven brand). `confirmed` as the speaker's own framework. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- **A "rational purchase" framing doesn't apply to a comfort-tier personal interior** — distinguishes "buying housing as a tool" (resale/liquidity-rational) from "buying comfort" (personal-taste, not chosen on cost-efficiency grounds, analogous to premium car or fine-dining choices); concludes that "investment design that resells for more" rarely holds true, and that tying interior budget to apartment price as a percentage is a myth since material/labor costs don't vary by the apartment's own price/location. `confirmed` as the speaker's stated view. **Third independent source now corroborating this theme**, alongside `yt_DsdLa87Acz4` and `yt_OrKB6uSKRyk` — no longer just one practitioner's opinion. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- **Small "nuance" decisions accumulate into large budget swings** — worked example: laminate+tile vs. engineered-wood+tile look similar from a few meters away but differ ~5× in material cost and in installation junction detail (floating laminate needs a visible metal transition profile; glued engineered wood allows a near-invisible cork expansion joint); a single such decision can add 10,000–15,000 RUB/m², and dozens of comparable decisions can compound to 200,000–400,000 RUB/m² total. `confirmed`. Extends this store's existing "vague smeta entry hides scope" theme to a materials/detailing example. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- **A quality-tolerance classification (Q1–Q4) exists for construction execution standards** — GOST/SP code allows ~3mm/meter wall-verticality deviation; one studio's own working standard is ±0.5mm per 2 linear meters (called "Q4"), keeping a 3m-tall wall's total deviation under ~1mm instead of ~9mm. `confirmed` as the speaker's stated standard; the Q1-Q4 classification's existence as an external standard is not independently verified here. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- **Overspending on trendy radiators at the expense of finishing the project is a real, cited failure mode**, not a hypothetical caution — one anecdote describes ~300,000 RUB spent on radiators alone leaving insufficient funds to finish the renovation; the budget-tier alternative is to keep/re-plumb and repaint developer-supplied stock radiators (cited installation-adjacent reference: ~350–500 RUB/m² for decent stock units) rather than buying new ones. `single-account`, but a specific, concrete number, not a vague warning. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- **A "budget premium-look" technique**: foil-wrapped MDF/particleboard panel combined with porcelain tile can read as real wood/stone at a fraction of the cost (~40,000 RUB materials in the source's own anecdote, where a tenant paying 500,000 RUB/month rent reportedly mistook the panel for real wood and stone). For higher/premium segments the same speaker instead recommends painted walls with applied molding/trim profiles. `single-account`, but a concrete, reusable cost-saving mechanism. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- **Drywall (GKL) ceilings crack over time from ordinary building settling, and additionally from high-floor building sway in tall buildings** — a second, independent reason (distinct from the already-recorded flood-containment argument) favoring a stretch ceiling over drywall, per one practitioner's stated experience across multiple new-construction properties. `single-account`. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- **A building that prohibits facade-mounted outdoor AC units (e.g. for aesthetic/street-facing reasons) forces much longer refrigerant-line runs to a permitted location** (e.g. a courtyard-facing wall) — the AC model chosen must be explicitly rated for that longer run length, or risks compressor overheating and premature failure from running an undersized-for-the-distance unit. `confirmed`, a genuine technical mechanism from a specific project the source describes, not just a regulatory anecdote. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- **AC warranty structure**: buying equipment and installation through the same company commonly bundles a 5-year warranty covering both equipment and installation, for most brand tiers (cited: Mitsubishi Electric, Daikin, Electrolux) — except the cheapest no-name budget tier, which the source says carries only a 1-year *equipment* warranty (the installation warranty itself remains 5 years regardless of equipment tier). Rated minimum lifespan for quality brands cited as 10 years. `single-account`, retailer's own claim about his own stock/warranty terms. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- **Bed-clearance ergonomics**: minimum walkway around a bed is 60 cm (bare minimum, rarely comfortable), 90 cm recommended where space allows, measured from the foot of the bed to the nearest obstruction (e.g. a TV stand). Standard bed length ≈220 cm; mattress widths commonly come in 20 cm increments (120/140/160/180 cm). `confirmed`, concrete planning numbers — note the source has its own internal inconsistency on the "+5cm vs +20cm over mattress size" sizing rule, already flagged in Risks/Gaps, which doesn't affect these underlying clearance/dimension figures. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- **Direct-hire "universalist" craftsmen (bypassing a managed company) can bring construction labor to ~20,000–30,000 RUB/m²**, well below this same source's own managed-company rate (60,000 RUB/m²) — but requires the client to act as their own site manager (~4 hrs/day estimated) and to simplify the design (avoid hidden doors, built-in baseboards, paint-ready drywall walls) so a generalist crew can execute it without specialist-level finish work. `single-account`, but unusually relevant to a self-managed renovation plan specifically, and a concrete number. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- **Install a temporary used toilet with a flexible connector on day one of rough plumbing work**, alongside the plumber's very first visit — avoids improvising (using a neighbor's bathroom) during the weeks the real bathroom is unusable. `confirmed`, concrete and specific, and genuinely new practical content — no prior source in this store covered this. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- **Hire an independent new-build handover inspection service rather than self-inspecting** when accepting keys on a new-construction apartment — the speaker's own experience specifically caught window-related defects a self-inspection would likely have missed. `confirmed`, own stated practice. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- **White/light-colored wallpaper over plain plastered walls needs a full white-priming pass first**, since underlying plaster is grey/tan and will visibly show through unprimed white wallpaper; colored/patterned wallpaper is more forgiving of this and doesn't strictly need the same priming step. `confirmed`, a concrete, checkable finishing-sequence rule not previously in this store. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- **A documented, disciplined self-managed renovation still overshot its own stated budget target by ~30%** ($10,000 target vs. ≈$12,957 actual) despite unusually granular line-by-line cost tracking — a real-world illustration that itemization alone doesn't guarantee hitting a target figure, relevant context for this project's own buffer assumption. `confirmed`, directly stated by the source. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- **Riser (стояк) water shutoffs for plumbing work are a paid service through the building's management company**, not an informal/free shutoff — budget an explicit small line item per riser (larger apartments with two risers pay twice). `confirmed`. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]

### Doors / Trim (new topic area, added 2026-07-31)

- **Door unit (дверной блок) has five components**: leaf (полотно), frame (коробка), jamb extensions (доборы), casing/trim (наличники), hardware (фурнитура). Standard leaf height 2.0 m; standard leaf widths 60/70/80/90 cm. `confirmed`. [source: yt_ludvy76HGSU]
- **A 60 cm-wide leaf will not pass a 60 cm-wide appliance (e.g. washing machine) through a 60 cm door**, despite matching nominal widths — the frame's rebate/stop (прихлоп, ~1 cm) plus the ~2-3 mm perimeter installation gap reduce the true clear opening below the nominal leaf width. `confirmed`, concrete non-obvious mechanism. [source: yt_ludvy76HGSU]
- **Adding a bottom threshold/rebate (порог с прихлопом) for extra soundproofing is not recommended** — modern interior door soundproofing is already adequate (the audible gain is imperceptible), while a ~3 cm threshold becomes a lasting trip hazard. `confirmed` as speaker's recommendation. [source: yt_ludvy76HGSU]
- **Telescopic jamb extensions (доборы) are superior to standard flat-board doborы** — they slide into a channel in the frame and self-adjust to an uneven wall or a slightly twisted frame, producing better junctions. `confirmed` as speaker's stated preference. [source: yt_ludvy76HGSU]
- **Door installation-plane offset must match the swing direction** — the frame should sit flush with the wall on the side the door swings into (clean foam joint under a flat casing), with the full reveal treatment on the opposite side. Getting this backwards causes the open door to strike/split the opening's corner, limits the swing to ~90° instead of ~95°, and is linked to more serious injuries. `confirmed`, safety-relevant mechanism. [source: yt_ludvy76HGSU]
- **Sliding interior doors have materially worse soundproofing than hinged doors**, even when hidden behind a false wall — a hinged door seals on 3 of 4 sides via its frame rebate; a basic sliding door has essentially no seal. `single-account`, ~80% of the speaker's own sliding-door clients complained about sound, complaints stopped when switched to hinged. [source: yt_1YiVgB9jqyU]
- **Sliding-door mechanisms (rollers, stoppers, soft-close dampers) commonly begin failing en masse ~3-4 years after installation**, and repair requires disassembling substantial finished work (furniture, ceiling, flooring, part of the wall) to reach the hidden mechanism. `single-account`, but the speaker's stated primary reason for abandoning sliding doors in his own practice after using them heavily from ~2009. [source: yt_1YiVgB9jqyU]
- **Sliding doors are appropriate as a narrow exception**: a private ensuite bathroom/shower serving only 1-2 people (e.g. attached to a bedroom for a couple) where a hinged door's swing arc has no viable path — soundproofing/reliability downsides are tolerable specifically because (a) only a small, intimate user group is affected and (b) usage frequency is low. Outside this case, the channel's standing recommendation is not to use sliding interior doors. `confirmed`, real project example plus explicit decision logic. [source: yt_x4TlTKt211Q]
- **A doorless, casing-trimmed opening between two rooms still needs a small wall stub (~10 cm, "при стенок") preserved at each outer corner** so casing can terminate cleanly on both faces of the wall — removing it (without checking with the design technologist first) leaves one face impossible to trim cleanly. `confirmed`, real jobsite mistake-and-fix example. [source: yt_c4b7iyg8v5U]
- **A full-length ("growth") mirror can be mounted directly on an entrance door or entry-area wardrobe leaf** when there's no separate wall space for one — described as cheaper than buying the door and a separate mirror individually, and the entryway makeup/grooming zone is the correct place for it (not the living room or a guest WC). Never hang a mirror directly opposite another mirror (infinite-corridor reflection effect). `confirmed`. [source: yt_b2rxvzj55Q0]
- **Entrance door minimum functional clear/light opening width: ~74 cm (740 mm)**, stated by the speaker as the practical floor below which passing through with furniture (e.g. a non-collapsible armchair) becomes impractical — this floor should take priority over cosmetic rules (e.g. casing standoff) when a hard site constraint (neighboring wall, embedded electrical conduit) forces a choice. `confirmed`, concrete numeric floor from a real constrained case. [source: yt_D2Qz6s8eNoU]
- **The ~10 cm casing-standoff rule is a default, not absolute** — a real documented case kept casing nearly flush to a wall because the opposite directions were blocked by a neighboring apartment's wall and a fixed electrical conduit box; the explicit prioritization rule given is "functional door width over cosmetic junction detail" when the two conflict. `confirmed`. [source: yt_D2Qz6s8eNoU]
- **Relocating a developer-installed entrance door opening (light partition wall) is generally low-complexity**: cut/rebuild the wall section, weld a steel angle-iron frame (~8 mm stock) around the new opening for reinforcement, backfill with cement-sand mortar, weld the door frame to the angle-iron, plaster to conceal it, and finish reveals with standard interior-door jamb-extension panels to match. Cited downsides limited to needing anti-vandal stairwell-plaster repair; no cost figures given for the work itself. `single-account`, real project (~30 cm shift, light aerated-concrete partition — may not generalize to load-bearing walls or larger shifts). [source: yt_oVf6AyxJjd0]
- **Interior door swing-direction rule (a coherent, safety-grounded 5-reason system)**: living-space doors (bedroom, living room, study, kids' room, kitchen, library) should open INWARD, for safety (fewer people pass through the room itself vs. the shared corridor), light-switch ergonomics (handle and switch on the same inside wall, push-to-enter keeps orientation in the dark), open-position convenience (parks against an interior wall vs. blocking a often-only-~1m-wide corridor), floor-stopper placement (inside the room vs. a corridor trip hazard), and closing convenience (reachable from the corridor). WC/bathroom doors (and small utility rooms/closets) should open OUTWARD, for space (bathrooms have less interior area than the corridor) and — the safety-critical reason — so an incapacitated occupant blocking the door from inside can still be reached without the door striking them. Quick heuristic given: if the light switch is inside the room, the door opens inward; if outside, it opens outward. `confirmed` as speaker's consistently-applied rule; speaker states <1% of his own projects use an inward-opening WC door for unexplained edge-case reasons. [source: yt_7Vd95idVXak]

### Doors / Trim — second batch (added 2026-07-31, non-Zemstandart channels)

- **Concealed/hidden-mount door installation cost is roughly double what the door's own material price suggests once fully itemized**: one company's own breakdown — base structural install (frame, concealed hinges, lock) 7,000–10,000 RUB, plus two-sided painting 5,000–6,000 RUB, plus flush-side wall-seam finishing 5,000–7,000 RUB, plus exposed-reveal finishing ≈7,000 RUB — commonly totals ≈30,000 RUB, roughly matching the ≈30,000 RUB material/kit floor for the door itself. `confirmed`, most granular concealed-door install breakdown in this store to date. [source: yt_yHmEQTqduDk]
- **Concealed door cost multiplier vs. classic door, independently stated**: 2–3× the price of an equivalent classic door once frame/hardware/prep/installation are counted, compounding to "hundreds of thousands of RUB" across 5–6 doors in one apartment — design renders typically don't disclose this delta up front. `confirmed`, corroborates the itemized ~2× finding above from a second, unrelated company. [source: yt_ORkPwMJ-AzU]
- **Concealed doors require higher-spec wall preparation as a precondition**: unlike classic doors (whose casing hides minor wall unevenness), a concealed door reveals every deviation in the adjacent wall surface — installing one in an otherwise economy-tier renovation is described as a mismatch ("sport wheels on a car with a broken suspension"), and the added wall prep is itself an extra cost. `confirmed`, new mechanism for this store. [source: yt_ORkPwMJ-AzU]
- **Concealed doors can develop a visible crack at the wall-junction seam months/years after installation**, from ordinary building settling (worse in new-construction) — cosmetic/structural-motion, not a defect, but alarming to clients expecting a permanently flawless flush surface. `confirmed`, new mechanism, distinct from this store's existing flood-containment/settling-crack facts about ceilings. [source: yt_ORkPwMJ-AzU]
- **Concealed-door daily-wear visibility**: because the door reads as part of the wall rather than a separate framed object, fingerprints/scuffs/impacts are more visible than on a classic door (whose casing visually "contains" such marks) — worse with dark paint, kids, or pets. Independently demonstrated on camera by a second source: both dark and light painted concealed-door surfaces show a lasting fingerprint mark from a single bare touch. `confirmed`, two independent sources. [source: yt_ORkPwMJ-AzU, yt_-HKwxCBa40k]
- **Concealed doors have essentially no tolerance for fabrication/registration errors**: a leaf/frame strike-plate size mismatch is invisible until final installation; one documented real example required ~1.5 months of rework to correct. `confirmed`, real jobsite example on camera, extends this store's existing "concealed doors don't forgive errors" theme with a concrete rework-time figure. [source: yt_-HKwxCBa40k]
- **Concealed door + shadow-gap ("theневой") baseboard combination adds 1.5–2 months to a renovation's schedule**, largely because the matching shadow-gap baseboard alone requires up to 14 distinct installation operations vs. a simple nailed classic baseboard. `confirmed`, first concrete schedule-impact figures for concealed doors in this store (previously this store's concealed-door coverage was cost/technique only, not schedule). [source: yt_-HKwxCBa40k]
- **A concealed door is dimensioned assuming its matching shadow-gap baseboard specifically** — substituting a classic baseboard causes the leaf to strike/bind against it on opening, risking damage; this is a structural dependency, not just a style-matching preference. `confirmed`, extends the store's existing shadow-gap-baseboard cost-driver material (previously noted from `yt_yHmEQTqduDk`'s "designer draws a concealed door, expect a shadow-gap baseboard too" observation) into a hard technical constraint. [source: yt_-HKwxCBa40k]
- **Concealed-door installation logistics burden**: flooring must be scribed/fitted around the pre-installed door assembly (reverse of the normal classic-door sequence where flooring goes down first); frame boxes need active protection for the renovation's full remaining duration (often ~1 year) since they're installed at rough-in stage; leaves sometimes arrive from the manufacturer unfinished (need on-site painting/filing); a full apartment's leaves delivered at once creates a real on-site storage problem — best practice given is to have the manufacturer hold leaves in their own warehouse until final hang-stage. `confirmed`, five genuinely new logistics/sequencing facts, real jobsite examples shown on camera. [source: yt_-HKwxCBa40k]
- **Sliding-pocket door soundproofing weakness, independently corroborated a second time**: this store's existing sliding-door-soundproofing fact (from Zemstandart, `yt_1YiVgB9jqyU`) is now corroborated by a second, unrelated company, which independently ranks sliding-pocket doors as the clear soundproofing outsider among the three main mount types, for the same underlying reason (leaf anchored only at the top rail, minimal seal elsewhere) — recommends caution using sliding-pocket doors in bathrooms/WC and bedrooms. `confirmed`, now a two-source-corroborated fact rather than a single practitioner's opinion. [source: yt_yHmEQTqduDk]
- **Sliding-pocket door mechanism fragility, independently corroborated with a second specific failure point**: this store's existing sliding-door mechanism-failure fact (rollers/dampers failing ~3-4 years in, per Zemstandart) is joined by a second company's account of a different specific fragile component — the opening-limiter/stopper inside the pocket cavity — which is awkward to reach/repair once damaged (some designs need a wall access hatch planned in for service). `confirmed`, complementary (not duplicate) mechanism. [source: yt_yHmEQTqduDk]
- **Interior door material/quality tiers (retail, by frame construction, distinct from mount type)**: cheapest MDF-film/cardboard-core doors (not repairable if damaged — must replace whole leaf) → "eco-veneer" (экошпон) doors (better pine frame + MDF panels wrapped in polypropylene film — the "eco"/veneer naming has no actual connection to real wood veneer or ecological materials, a marketing-naming distinction worth knowing) → solid-wood ("массив") doors (repairable, ~5,000 RUB professional restoration if scratched/damaged) → concealed doors with aluminum edge trim, itself two sub-tiers (cheap Chinese aluminum trim on a cheap pine/cardboard core, explicitly warned against vs. full-aluminum-frame construction that won't warp and is field-repairable by touch-up paint/polish). **Repairability, not just up-front price, is the key cross-tier quality differentiator.** `confirmed`. [source: yt_COhFXPyfXxM]
- **Door hardware (hinges + handle + lock) should not be bought cheap even on an otherwise budget door** — a quality hardware set costs ~5,000–7,000 RUB and is stated to last ~10 years; low-priced hardware is described as unreliable by default. `confirmed`, new numeric anchor specifically for hardware (previously this store's door-cost figures were mostly whole-set totals). [source: yt_COhFXPyfXxM]
- **Enamel-painted vs. film-wrapped (PVC/polypropylene/"eco-veneer") door surface finish — a distinct axis from frame material or mount type**: enamel can be locally sanded/filled/repainted if scratched or chipped (inexpensive, effectively invisible after repair) and can be fully repainted a different color later; film cannot be locally repaired at all (a scratch requires replacing the whole panel or leaf) and cannot be painted over. A manufacturing debris fleck trapped under film stays permanently visible as a raised defect; the same defect under wet enamel can be sanded flush and repainted. Film-wrapped doors reportedly build up static charge and attract more airborne dust than enamel, especially visible at seams/corners (worse near a busy road with frequently open windows). Day-to-day cleaning performance is not a differentiator between the two. One stated drawback of enamel: a cheap 1–2-coat application can show minor "orange peel" texture at an angle — a manufacturing-quality issue, not inherent to enamel as a material. `confirmed`. [source: yt_WMc_AjUqQ-4]
- **Entrance-door security-spec claims can be directly contradictory between competing self-interested retailers** — one Moscow-area retailer's own product line frames thin steel (1.2 mm) and standard locks as fully adequate, citing a secondhand press statistic (Moscow burglaries down "35x in 13 years," "199 city-wide in 11 months," mostly unlocked-door incidents, not forced entry) to argue thicker-metal/high-security doors are oversold marketing; a competing manufacturer in the same batch sells a full tiered ladder up to 2 mm steel / Italian locks / 65 dB soundproofing at up to 380,000 RUB. **Neither framing is adopted as this store's own security guidance** — both are self-interested claims (one favors selling cheaper stock, the other favors selling the expensive tiers), recorded side by side as an illustration of this store's existing market-data-vs-tier-steering guidance in action. `unverified` on both sides. [source: yt_Mfzce0Qm4HM, yt_eM6HMMmumhE]

### Electrical / Lighting (new topic area, added 2026-08-04)

> All 29 entries in this subsection come from one channel (Zemstandart / Alexey Zemskov) — `single-account`/`unverified` per this store's corroboration rule throughout, even where a claim repeats across several of the channel's own videos. Feeds `12_Engineering_and_Systems/Electrical_and_Lighting.md`.

**Mounting heights & positioning**

- **Switch height is a fixed 90 cm from finished floor, independent of occupant height** — a repeated on-camera demonstration (four people, heights 150–185 cm) shows the resting hand-drop height from standing is essentially constant across adults; switch center is placed 15 cm from the rough door-opening edge (moving it to 10 cm causes the switch to conflict with door casing). A household with unusually tall occupants may use 95 cm instead of 90 cm. `confirmed`, repeated demonstration. [source: yt_uLJGsTfTj3A, yt_l_rjjPlPkRo]
- **Bedside electrical points scale off furniture, not a fixed number**: bedside lamp switch ~120 cm (headboard-mounted) or ~70 cm (nightstand-mounted) from floor; bedside outlets ~70 cm; an over-bed pendant/lamp cable drop at ~100 cm; a formula combining false-wall offset + curtain-zone clearance + nightstand width + half the bed's width sets the horizontal position. `confirmed` as the channel's own design formula. [source: yt_uLJGsTfTj3A, yt_VK8rCdChdbY]
- **Countertop outlets sit 110 cm from finished floor by default**, and shift by the same delta as the countertop height when a non-standard (e.g. +10 cm) countertop is planned — measured from floor, not from the not-yet-installed countertop, because the countertop's exact future height is harder to plan against reliably. Window-slope outlets are the explicit exception, measured from the slope/sill instead of the floor; ceiling-referenced points (e.g. AC unit alignment) use the ceiling as datum. `confirmed`. [source: yt_uLJGsTfTj3A, yt_12o621100MQ]
- **A small set of point types should track occupant eye level rather than the fixed heights above**: video-intercom monitors and underfloor-heating thermostats, both read visually while standing/using them, unlike switches which are operated by feel. `confirmed`. [source: yt_uLJGsTfTj3A]
- **Bedroom electrical points around a bed must reference the bed's own center, never a corner or doorway** — a genuinely new exception to the default corner/opening-reference rule already recorded above (from `yt_12o621100MQ`): introduce an explicit intermediate reference point ("center of bed") and measure every point around the bed horizontally from it. Reasoning: visual symmetry relative to the bed itself is what a person actually perceives — points that read as clean round numbers from a corner/doorway can still look visibly crooked if asymmetric relative to the bed (e.g. 5cm off-center one side, 11cm the other), and that bed-relative asymmetry is far more noticeable than corner-relative precision. `confirmed`, the single most novel rule from this source. [source: yt_ChXZrzgDrgs]
- **Electrical-point *content* notation is a separate concern from *coordinate* notation** (the latter already covered above): a standard abbreviation legend — R = outlet, V = switch, ТВ = TV cable output, УТП = UTP/ethernet output, with a leading digit for quantity (e.g. "2R+ТВ+УТП" = a 4-module point: 2 outlets + TV + UTP) — keeps a point's description compact without losing precision. **Critical rule: a point's content description must stay directly attached to its location marker on the drawing, never split into a separate reference table** — a real cited failure (a table-row mismatch, row 7 confused with row 8) resulted in an as-built switch where two outlets were specified, discovered only after the client paid for rework. `confirmed`, real cited failure with financial consequence. [source: yt_ChXZrzgDrgs]
- **Corner-labeling and default horizontal-reference-point conventions independently restated ~2.5 years later, same channel**: rooms shown top-down, corners labeled alphabetically clockwise from the top-left (А, Б, В, Г...), horizontal reference defaults to nearest opening or nearest corner (whichever is closer), stated explicitly as a project-wide fallback rule to reduce visual clutter. `confirmed`, same-channel restatement (not independent corroboration — shows a stable practice over time within one company, not a second source). [source: yt_ChXZrzgDrgs, yt_12o621100MQ]

**Cable sizing, circuits & panel design**

- **Cable-gauge-by-load table (copper, this channel's standard)**: 1.5 mm² for lighting circuits, 2.5 mm² for socket/outlet circuits, 4 mm² for ovens, 6 mm² for cooktops/high-draw appliances; a 4–6 mm² high-load circuit should never be split or branched partway. `confirmed`. [source: yt_JrClDJb8WTM]
- **Breaker curve selection**: curve B for socket circuits (tolerates appliance inrush current without nuisance-tripping), curve C for lighting and AC circuits; curve D is not recommended for residential use. Breaker amperage is paired to cable gauge (roughly 10A/1.5mm², 16A/2.5mm², 20A/4mm², 32A/6mm² in this channel's convention). `confirmed`, internally consistent with the circuit-formula fact below. [source: yt_JrClDJb8WTM, yt_ciXeqvKDKSI]
- **Panel circuit-count formula**: minimum 2 circuits per room (one lighting, one sockets), +1 dedicated circuit for AC; oven and cooktop each get their own dedicated circuit; wet rooms (bathroom/WC/kitchen) require an RCD/differential breaker. `confirmed`, consistent with the wire/breaker pairing table above. [source: yt_ciXeqvKDKSI]
- **Route electrical conduit above plumbing runs on the ceiling, not stacked on the floor** — avoids compounding conduit+pipe thickness inside the screed depth near door thresholds, a common source of a raised threshold lip. Conduit should clear adjacent walls by 15 cm minimum (20–25 cm preferred) for installation and future baguette/trim clearance. `confirmed`, guest electrician's stated practice. [source: yt_Onu15qOeWGA]
- **On walls slated for later acoustic insulation, recess socket boxes deeper than a standard wall during rough-in** — otherwise the finished box ends up proud of the insulated wall surface once the soundproofing layer is added (this generalizes an already-recorded fact about back-box offset in this store's Do's/Don'ts; here it's the recessed-box-depth version of the same underlying problem). [source: yt_Onu15qOeWGA]
- **Sample only the ground (yellow-green) conductor when checking an existing de-energized service-entry cable's gauge** — avoids disturbing live/neutral conductors unnecessarily; extending an undersized service cable uses crimp ferrule sleeves (to convert stranded wire to a solid-equivalent end before screw-terminal connections) and heat-shrink gel splice kits for any in-line joint. `confirmed`, procedural detail. [source: yt_alnV3DYCDlg]

**Temporary / construction-stage wiring**

- **Temporary (rough-in-stage) wiring uses a different, deliberately simpler spec than permanent wiring**: 2×2.5 mm² ungrounded (vs. permanent 3×1.5 mm² for lighting / 3×2.5 mm² for sockets, both grounded) — grounding is treated as unnecessary for the temporary stage. Install temp wiring after the design/electrical plan exists but before interior walls go up, and after ceiling priming (not before).
