- **Bathroom switch-usability pattern**: main-light switch at the doorway
  (hit naturally on the way out); a separate exhaust-fan switch near the
  toilet/sink (so a user doesn't need to stand and reach the door to
  trigger it) — never place the fan switch outside the bathroom itself.
- **The "gap under the tub for your feet" convention is explicitly
  obsolete** — tile should run flush to the floor line under the tub's
  front edge, sealed with grout, no reachable gap. A tile "step" ridge
  insert marketed as space-saving has no functional benefit either — just
  a dirt/grime collector.
- **Reject an unexplained "always 3 sockets near the sink" rule of
  thumb** — size actual socket count to genuine need instead.
- **Bedroom lighting-temperature consistency rule**: use at most 2-3
  lighting circuits per room, and ensure every fixture across those
  circuits shares the same color temperature — mixing temperatures across
  multiple circuits reads as visually chaotic. Extends this store's
  existing three-layer-lighting concept (`yt_OCjVd5HOcac`) with a
  concrete circuit-count ceiling and explicit same-temperature
  constraint.
- **Window sash sizing must account for the finished floor height before
  the window order is placed** — the sash's lowest swing position must
  clear the finished floor covering with margin, or a sagging sash will
  eventually scrape it. New sequencing rule (order-of-operations across
  two trades).
- **A tilt-and-turn sash's scissor hardware can be damaged by improper
  closing technique** (opening past the scissor-disengagement point, then
  pushing it not-quite-closed causes repeated impact damage to the slope)
  — a misuse-driven failure mode distinct from this store's existing
  manufacturing-defect mechanisms for tilt-and-turn hardware
  (`yt_XFrmhhM1ogg`).

### Kids Room (new topic area, added 2026-08-14)

- **Avoid the phrase "детский дом" (children's home/orphanage) in client-facing language for a kids'-house/loft-bed structure — use "детский домик" instead.** `confirmed`, `single-account`. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **A kids'-house/loft-bed structure (детский домик) must be planned and wired into finish electrical from the start of a renovation, not added after** — it's described as too tightly integrated with finish electrical (independent switches at each sleeping spot) to retrofit cleanly once the rest of the renovation is complete. `confirmed`, `single-account`, a specific sequencing rule. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **Provision two independently-controlled switches near each sleeping spot in a shared kids' room**: one pass-through switch for the room's main light, one separate switch for a personal night-light — avoids one child needing to negotiate with a sibling over the shared main light. `confirmed`, demonstrated in the company's own project. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **Deliberately leave the middle section of a shared two-child wardrobe unused as a neutral buffer zone between each child's own storage area** — an explicit, acknowledged storage-capacity tradeoff made specifically to reduce recurring sibling territorial conflict. `confirmed` as stated design rationale, `single-account`. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]
- **When building a wardrobe with an intentionally-unused buffer section, add its dedicated interior-lighting electrical cable during the same install** — stated as impossible to add cleanly once the wardrobe itself is installed, extending the general "wire before you build" theme also present elsewhere in this channel's content. `confirmed`, `single-account`. [source: yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU]

### Smart Home / Switch-Logic — "Stupidest Smart House" Critique (new topic area, added 2026-08-14)

- **Switches should be positioned so their function is inferable purely by physical proximity/logic**, without a printed legend — demonstrated on camera with an unrehearsed test: a staff designer correctly guessed which of four switches controlled which of four light fixtures purely from layout logic, with no prior briefing. `confirmed`, directly demonstrated. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Multi-button "piano"-style scene-control switch panels (up to ~64-68 preset scenes) are explicitly scoped by this company to business-class-and-above budgets, not comfort-class** — framed as disproportionately complex/expensive relative to benefit at the comfort price point; comfort-class default is individual, logically-placed switches instead. **New tier-scoping nuance** on switch/control hardware choice, distinct from this store's existing physical-placement/wiring switch rules. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **"Smart door" is not a distinct product — buy a plain door and a separate smart lock instead of a pre-integrated bundle**, reported to save "at least 3x." Regardless of price tier, fingerprint/app smart locks are reported to occasionally fail to open reliably; the phone-app fallback is reported to work in practice. `confirmed` as stated company advice, `single-account`. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Demonstrated voice-assistant (Alice/Yandex) command-batching limitation**: a single combined command covering two actions ("open the curtains and turn off the light") reliably failed to execute either action in a repeated on-camera test; the same two actions issued as separate sequential commands worked. A partial-position curtain command ("close 50%") also worked. `confirmed`, directly demonstrated — genuinely new voice-assistant reliability caveat for this store. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Rule: any smart/voice-controlled lighting circuit must retain a parallel physical switch** — without one, a malfunction forces using the branch breaker in the electrical panel as the only fallback. `confirmed`, reinforces this store's existing physical-switch-duplication principle from an independent source. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **"Duty/night light" (дежурное освещение) — continuous low-level lighting kept on in hallways and bathrooms/WCs for night-time use, without needing full lighting.** Explicit search-term note from the speaker: "duty light" is internal company jargon and won't find the product online — search "подсветка лестницы" (stair lighting) instead to find the correct fixture type. `confirmed`, checkable sourcing tip. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Milled/routed cabinet and door handles preferred over push-pull mechanisms (unreliable release, demonstrated on camera) and over protruding standard handles (snag/damage adjacent surfaces on swing-open, real collision-injury risk)** — stated as this company's now-default choice. `confirmed` as company stated preference with mechanism given, `single-account`. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Low-voltage/server cabinet or panel must include ventilation** or accumulated equipment heat risks failure over time — never buy furniture with a built-in ventilation grille for this (≈3x markup vs. a plain cabinet + a separately-sourced generic grille cut in by an installer). `confirmed` as stated design rule/purchasing tactic, `single-account`. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Ultra-thin/flush-mount TVs use a single combined power+signal UTP connection, locking the TV type into the design-project stage before rough-in is finalized** — if the buyer later switches to a standard TV, the wall's single UTP outlet cannot supply a standard TV's normal power+signal inputs; described as not reversible once walls are closed. Ultra-thin TVs quoted at roughly 3x a comparable standard TV's price. `confirmed` mechanism, `single-account`, `unverified` price multiple. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]
- **Comfort/business/elite labor-only price bands restated (not a new corroborating data point)**: comfort 25,000-45,000 / business 50,000-85,000 / elite 90,000-150,000 RUB/m², materials excluded — identical to the figures already recorded from `yt_7VfZIYGUrTo` and `yt_Gp5Lr20SrtU`, all same company within the same week. [source: yt_n_1gnhHM184]

### "Best Solutions" Mixed-Topic Compilation Batch (playlist `PLpZakfG8U02uR3KlsxtWXyChOETu-o-Aj`, added 2026-08-15)

All items below are `single-account` (same channel) and `ASR-uncertain` unless noted, given this sub-cluster's generally degraded transcript quality relative to most sources already in this store.

- **Wi-Fi access-point placement in reinforced-concrete apartments**: reinforced-concrete interior walls significantly attenuate Wi-Fi signal; a large apartment is described as needing roughly 3 access points for reliable whole-unit coverage, with at least one hidden inside a ceiling/soffit detail rather than left exposed. **Genuinely new topic for this store** — no prior source addresses networking/Wi-Fi equipment placement. `ASR-uncertain`, treat the "3 access points" figure as one rough worked example, not a general rule. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Dedicated circuit for network/router equipment**, with an accessible point to power-cycle it. `ASR-uncertain`, mechanism unclear. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Dedicated circuit for a child's room** to avoid overloading a single outlet/circuit. `ASR-uncertain`. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Lost-key cost-saving tip**: replacing only the lock cylinder, not the whole lock/hardware set, restores security cheaply. No price given. `ASR-uncertain`. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Mirror lighting should match the room's main light source's color temperature**, so a person's reflected appearance matches how they'll look under the room's general lighting. `ASR-uncertain`. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Closet/wardrobe interior shelf depth rule of thumb**: roughly 40-50 cm. Reasoning not reconstructable from this transcript. `ASR-uncertain`, treat as approximate. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Bathroom grab bar/safety rail near the shower or tub** to prevent slip injuries — general safety point, no installation detail recoverable. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Kitchen cooking-zone sightline rule**: orient the work zone so the person cooking can turn and see the room without fully turning their back to the stove. `ASR-uncertain`, mechanism not fully reconstructable. [source: yt_LCb8ix8Bl64]
- **Single-loop ("единым контуром") underfloor heating under laminate flooring carries a roughly 50/50 buckling-failure risk**, per the practitioner's own stated recent-years experience — quartz-vinyl tile mitigates but doesn't eliminate the risk, especially in irregularly-shaped rooms. **Safety/defect-relevant, genuinely new explicit failure-rate heuristic for this store.** `confirmed` as stated practitioner risk assessment, `single-account`, `unverified` as an industry statistic. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Voltage-protection relay (реле напряжения), now recommended on every panel**: one relay per phase (1 for single-phase service, 3 for three-phase). [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Server/network closet convention**: incoming twisted-pair, fiber, and TV-signal cabling all terminate in one small closet/panel housing the TV patch panel, router, and NAS, then redistribute to the whole apartment from there. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Electric towel warmers preferred over hydronic (water-fed) ones "without genuine necessity"** — stated rationale: trivial electricity cost vs. near-certain eventual water-line leak and its repair cost to the unit and the unit below. `single-account` stated practice/opinion. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Always pair a hygiene shower/bidet with at least a small sink**, even in a tight bathroom, so hands can be washed without touching a dirty door handle or walking to another room's sink. Genuinely new pairing rule for this store. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Avoid installed audio wall-outlet plates for speaker wiring — leave capped bare wire stubs behind the wall instead**, so removed speakers don't leave a permanent visible unused outlet; extra connector pairs also mildly degrade audio signal quality. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Paintable, texture-free wallpaper ("обои под покраску") allows invisible local spot-repainting** (even paint absorption vs. a painted-plaster wall's visible patch marks) — also the correct product choice for a Venetian-plaster *look*, using a wallpaper that imitates the texture rather than real plaster. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Trim all openings/arches/portals — including a joined loggia's reveal — with interior-door casing extensions ("доборы"), never natural stone/brick veneer**, which doesn't join cleanly with a standard baseboard. Cited cost ≈5x a ~2,000 RUB PVC-corner-trim baseline, still judged good value for the visual-quality gain. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Vacuum-cleaner outlet mounting height ≈1.1 m** — avoids bending to plug in, and the cord tends to release cleanly rather than yank the plug when dragged, versus a floor-level outlet. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Ethernet run to every TV location is standard regardless of Wi-Fi**, because Wi-Fi is not considered reliable enough for consistently high-quality video streaming. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Switch must be on the same side of a doorway as the door handle** — stated as a firm, non-negotiable ergonomic rule, with a real on-camera correction example of a misplaced switch. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Furniture assembly is outside a renovation contractor's standard scope; baseboard/trim fitting around furniture and built-in kitchen IS standard scope**, performed after furniture placement — a useful scope-of-work boundary for a self-managed project sequencing its own trades. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Socket-to-sink minimum-distance concern framed by the practitioner as a largely outdated myth for modern IP-rated sockets** (vs. genuinely poor 1960s aluminum-contact sockets) — `single-account`, `unverified` electrical-safety opinion, not a cited code standard; do not adopt as verified fact. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **A kitchen niche will almost always become an appliance "parking spot" (multicooker/kettle/coffee maker) regardless of its intended storage use** — default to adding a couple of outlets to any kitchen niche. [source: yt_nFWxi3zbsNo]
- **Never use polypropylene water-supply pipe — use standard plastic pipe with reliable fittings instead.** Firm, repeated practitioner position, justified anecdotally by claimed repeated on-camera rework of failed polypropylene systems. `single-account`, `unverified` industry-wide claim (no failure mechanism/standard cited) — genuinely new pipe-material warning for this store. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Factory-assembled, pre-tested electrical panels preferred over field-assembled ones**: factory units are load-tested, defective units are culled, and they ship with a manufacturer's warranty plus a product passport/certificate of conformity — none available from an on-site-assembled panel. Genuinely new panel-sourcing quality rule, complementary to this store's existing hired-electrician vetting signals. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Shoe-dryer outlets**: install two outlets near shoe storage specifically for electric shoe-dryer inserts. Genuinely new appliance-specific outlet-provisioning rule. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Server-closet circuit-separation detail** (extends the `yt_nFWxi3zbsNo` server-closet convention): of 4 outlets in the closet, 3 share one circuit and the 4th (dedicated to the NAS) is on a separate circuit, so rebooting the router doesn't also reboot network-attached storage. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Leak-detection sensor should sit a few centimeters above true floor level and never be glued down**, so it can be dried with a hairdryer and easily restored to service after a flooding event rather than pried up. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Windowsill-drilled directional-vent retrofit for window condensation**: if condensation persists after wide-windowsill installation blocks radiator warm air from reaching the glass, drill a hole through the sill opposite the center of each glass unit and insert a directional grille to channel warm air onto the lower glass. New concrete retrofit technique, beyond this store's existing "installation quality is the primary condensation cause" heuristic. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Wall-panel (PVC/decorative cladding) corner-forming technique without separate corner trim**: cut a groove in the panel's back edge, then repeatedly heat the cut line and wipe before folding around interior corners (heating required); exterior corners can fold cold if enough backing material is removed during the cut. Saves cost/time versus separate corner-trim pieces. Genuinely new fabrication technique for this store. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Loggia climate/lighting controls should be mounted in the door reveal/opening, not deep inside the loggia** — faster access, avoids stepping onto a cold unheated floor to reach the control, and status is visible from the warm side. Extends this store's existing loggia-control-placement content with a specific ergonomic rationale. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Custom furniture sourced from the same supplier as the kitchen cabinetry, for stylistic cohesion** — costs more than off-the-shelf furniture. `tier-steering`: company recommending its own bespoke-sourcing option; a cheaper independently-sourced alternative exists for a self-managed buyer. [source: yt_U56YIEA3-U4]
- **Prefer a patterned/speckled finish over a uniform dark/monotone color for a countertop or flooring surface** — pattern/contrast conceals dust, water spots, and footprints that a plain color shows every mark of; a real client case is cited where an imposed dark monotone countertop proved high-maintenance and is being replaced. Extends this store's existing flooring-pattern-hides-dirt principle to countertops. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Two separate, smaller (~260 L-class) refrigerators instead of one large unit**: cheaper than an equivalent-capacity single large fridge, allows food-category separation, and lets one unit be powered off entirely (e.g. while traveling) to save electricity. Genuinely new appliance-provisioning heuristic. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Microwave-over-oven appliance column should use lift-up (not hinged-down) cabinet doors** to avoid head-bumping when opened. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Cove/indirect ceiling lighting via a stretch-ceiling-integrated LED profile** (shaped edge profile with a translucent insert concealing the LED strip) replaces an open, dust-collecting light-strip channel; RGB/dimmable, ≈30,000 RUB added per room. Genuinely new lighting technique + price point, distinct from this store's existing (dust-exposed) perimeter-LED-baguette-channel content. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Remote/electronic entrance locks fail open (auto-unlock) when the battery becomes critically low**, as a stated safety default — meaning battery neglect is a real security exposure, not just an inconvenience. No ordinary lock (electronic or mechanical) resists a skilled break-in for more than ~30 seconds except a concealed lock, which can only be defeated by slow, loud mechanical grinding. Extends this store's existing smart-lock content with a security/reliability trade-off analysis. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Wardrobe/cabinet-box installer marking protocol to protect concealed wiring before a later stretch-ceiling install**: photograph the installed box, have the client independently photograph it, and attach a physical note stating the exact distance from the box's underside to the concealed cable run plus a maximum safe fastener length (35-40mm) — so a later stretch-ceiling crew doesn't drive a screw through hidden cabling. Genuinely new cross-trade coordination/documentation protocol. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Bathroom tile-finish selection heuristic**: choose a surface that hides water/soap spots but still shows toothpaste splatter — not so glossy every water droplet shows, not so matte grime disappears into the texture. Distinct from this store's existing tile-*layout* (cut-piece sizing) rules. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Hygienic-shower/bidet-sprayer plumbing without a wall shutoff valve, worked mechanism**: the sprayer head has no reliable shutoff hardware of its own, so constant line pressure risks an eventual burst/leak; shutoff instead routes through a button on the sprayer's wall-mounted holder (a "second circuit"), combined with a pre-set in-wall thermostatic mixing valve so no separate temperature control is needed at the sprayer. Trade-off: slightly less convenient docking/undocking. Genuinely new plumbing-mechanism detail, distinct from this store's existing hygienic-shower handedness-placement fact. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Joining a loggia increases total heat-loss surface area versus leaving it unjoined**, so the combined room runs somewhat cooler — fully compensable with normal underfloor heating and building heating, not a reason to avoid joining. Extends this store's existing loggia-join heat-loss content. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Renovation-budget spending-sequencing rule**: fully fund and complete concealed/"fundamental" work first (wiring, plumbing, tile, windows), since redoing any of it later is far costlier than doing it right the first time; defer non-structural, purchasable-later items instead (stretch ceilings can be added weeks/months post-move-in, door casings and doors themselves can wait until last, a kitchen can be delayed up to roughly six months). Distinct from this store's existing "don't cut cost on concealed systems" heuristic (`yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw`) — adds the *timing/sequencing* dimension. `single-account`. [source: yt_FO9mQWUeSuA]
- **Bathtub protection during rough work: plywood over the tub, rim masked with several layers of painter's tape underneath** — not just plastic sheeting; a real workaround warning is also recorded: crews sometimes flip dirty plywood over to look clean for a client/inspector visit rather than replace it, so the taped rim protects the tub even when the visible plywood has been "gamed." [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Floor drain/duct outlet grilles must be installed strictly level** — an uneven grille conflicts with surrounding flooring installation and looks/feels wrong underfoot. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Bathtub rim must sit recessed 1.5-3cm below the adjacent tile/wall surface, never flush** — a flush installation degrades the grout/sealant line faster over time. Genuinely new numeric installation rule. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Relief/textured tile forces a trade-off with perfectly mitered 45° inside corners** — no installer achieves a truly clean inside corner with relief-textured tile; choose either perfect corners or textured tile, not both. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **The structural ceiling behind a stretch-ceiling fabric must always be painted** (any color) so nothing sheds onto the underside of the stretch-ceiling fabric over time. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **On-site CCTV is installed to supervise crew behavior, not as a client-facing feature** — crews reportedly work more carefully and professionally when filmed; footage also protects workers from later disputed blame. `single-account`. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Plumbing access-hatch design principle**: sized/positioned for full "revision" access so any single fitting/valve can be removed and replaced individually without disassembling the surrounding assembly — not a decorative feature. Extends this store's general utility-concealment-box sizing heuristic with mechanism detail. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **A full supply+exhaust ventilation unit can consume a large share of ceiling height**, often forcing partial ceiling drops in adjacent rooms too — budget for lost height/cost in more than one room. **Sequence duct-routing subcontractors before finalizing or photographing planned electrical wiring layouts** — ad hoc duct routing reportedly overrides an already-neat electrical layout regardless of planning. Genuinely new cost/space trade-off + cross-trade sequencing rule. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **A genuine "tropical"/rain shower head must protrude from the wall on a proper riser arm or be ceiling-mounted** — a horizontal hand-held-style hose connection is not a real tropical-shower installation. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **To-the-ceiling kitchen cabinetry combined with a stretch ceiling needs a purpose-built corner box** (sized to the cabinetry footprint, sometimes with integrated upper-cabinet lighting) so the stretch-ceiling baguette can attach to the box's edge while the kitchen mounts to/near the same box — extends this store's existing wardrobe-ceiling-box coordination principle to a second furniture type. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Pre-wire a dedicated outlet for a heated-seat/smart toilet before finish work**, rather than improvising a connection from a nearby sink outlet afterward. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Never change a door/opening height mid-project once construction has started** — real cost comparison: reworking apartment-wide openings costs ≈2 extra days with paintable wallpaper finish, but ≈2 extra *weeks* with painted walls or standard wallpaper. Reinforces this store's existing paintable-wallpaper preference from a rework-cost angle. [source: yt_ps_6QOFsmAY]
- **Coordinate entrance-door and interior-door trim material at time of purchase** — different manufacturers make each, so a mismatch is easy unless deliberately specified together up front. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Real regret case: cutting a designed built-in washing-machine countertop to save ≈15,000 RUB later cost ≈50,000 RUB to retrofit.** Concrete number reinforcing this store's design-integrity-vs-cost-cutting theme. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Every entryway needs a dedicated, separately-lit makeup-mirror zone**, distinct from a general full-length mirror (not adequately lit for grooming checks). [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Floor-drain slope-direction rule when the drain is offset from the wall**: slope toward the drain from both directions, not one uniform slope — a uniform slope leaves a low point away from the drain where water pools and mold forms. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Budget lifehack: hide network/server equipment in a cheap small wall-mounted box as an interim solution** before a proper built-in server-closet cabinet is affordable. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Window-desk warm-airflow eye-strain mechanism**: a convector's warm air blown into a seated person's face at a window desk dries the eyes and accelerates eyesight strain — a third reason (alongside condensation and constant daylight) to only build a window desk as a last resort. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Windowsill-desk height must be lowered to match a standard chair, with electrical points planned for that lowered position** — real regret case: a client cancelled the lowered-desk millwork mid-project without telling the designer, leaving pre-planned outlets visibly misplaced relative to the simplified windowsill actually built. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Frameless glass shower-divider real cleaning-time data point**: ≈1.5-2 hours to clean fingerprint/water streaks off one panel on move-in day, including an improvised cleaning tool for an inaccessible corner seam — concrete maintenance-burden evidence for the glass-vs-curtain shower-divider trade-off. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Hallway/passage switch topology, worked example**: a passage connecting 3 rooms needs at least 3 separate switches so no one has to backtrack to turn off a light behind them. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Never add a pass-through switch immediately outside a bathroom that already has its own 2 switches (main + duty light)** — unintuitive (flipped behind the person leaving) and risks confusing the switch group. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **A bathroom/WC needs its own duty/night light, not just the connecting hallway's** — cover all rooms needing duty lighting on one shared switch. Extends this store's existing duty-light content with the WC-specific gap. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Track-lighting (шинопровод) minimum busbar width for a room's main light source: 38mm** — most designers spec 20-25mm rails, which can only carry weaker fixtures inadequate as primary lighting. First track-lighting content in this store. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Avoid a light fixture with 3 fixed cycled color-temperature presets via repeated clicking** — every use requires cycling through unwanted tones; prefer a single-tone, single-mode fixture instead. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Unplanned late loggia join without 3D pre-modeling causes compounding junction defects, real case**: kitchen cabinetry met the new opening trim with zero gap (should have been offset ~40mm); the loggia-side countertop needed 3 recuts for not accounting for wall-cladding and trim thickness. **Numeric rule: a countertop must overhang the adjoining wall/trim assembly by a minimum of 40mm** to correctly seat trim up to ~20mm thick without appearing to float. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Bathroom leak-sensor count by floor-level choice**: a lowered bathroom floor needs at least 1 leak sensor; a level (flush) floor needs 2, since there's no natural low point guaranteeing single-sensor coverage. Extends this store's existing leak-sensor content. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Laminate-under-underfloor-heating risk, refined**: even in a small apartment, split laminate into separate zones/loops per room rather than one continuous loop, regardless of apartment size — quartz-vinyl remains safe in one continuous loop. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Curtain-pelmet channel + shadow-gap stretch-ceiling combination technique**: a specialized recessed curtain-rod pelmet channel that the stretch-ceiling fabric deliberately stops short of, so curtain-rod concealment and a shadow-gap "floating ceiling" effect (otherwise mutually exclusive) can coexist. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Vet a stretch-ceiling contractor by whether they leave access hatches near track-lighting transformers/drivers** — these fail periodically regardless of price tier; no access panel means full ceiling demolition/reinstall for every future repair (effectively a recurring "subscription fee"). [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Joining a loggia with flush flooring usually requires raising the loggia floor 10-15cm (≈90% of apartments)**, but occasionally the whole apartment's floor must be raised instead — real cited cost "several hundred thousand RUB," with a real downside: the finished floor can end up higher than the shared-corridor entry threshold, forcing a step down on exit. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Sliding-door (шкаф-купе) wardrobes are cheaper than framed/corpus wardrobes of the same footprint** — no back/top/bottom panel fabrication needed since the surrounding wall/niche does that job — plus more space-efficient and generally more reliable hardware. Always plan a ceiling box above a corpus wardrobe if chosen instead. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Temporary baseboard with pre-planned future cut zones**: install temp baseboard everywhere (even future-furniture walls) so the unit doesn't look unfinished during the year-plus gap before furniture arrives — but pre-mark the exact points a later furniture crew will need to cut, so they cut cleanly instead of tearing the baseboard/wallpaper at an arbitrary spot. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Plan TV-wall outlet position specifically behind the actual planned TV location**, not just generally on the TV wall — a real mistake shown produces visible cable "spaghetti" on day one. Extends this store's existing TV-cable-channel-provisioning content. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Kids-room door-swing exception (extends the store's existing ~95° default)**: an unusually wide ~180° swing is deliberate when a large wardrobe will occupy the space a normal door stop would use. Wardrobe interior lighting must always be planned in advance — frequently forgotten. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Fresh-air "breather" wall-placement trade-off, brand-agnostic**: surface-mounted near a window looks tech-forward but conflicts with curtain operation and wastes floor space; recessed into a technical niche avoids that but leaves permanently-visible imperfect exposed corners. Presented as the lesser evil vs. losing floor space. [source: yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ]
- **Two-component ("двухкомпонентные") radiator grilles**: an outer frame plus a magnetically-attached center insert that pops off alone for access (bleeding air, wiping down), unlike a standard fixed grille that's very hard to remove. Made to order by the same custom-fabrication shops as standard bespoke grilles. Designer (exposed, never concealed) radiators cost roughly 3x a standard radiator. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Stretch-ceiling light lines can now fully replace a room's main/central light fixture** — bright enough for both use and cleaning per the practitioner — but cost significantly more than a standard fixture, and a failed light line can require replacing the stretch ceiling itself to repair. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Paintable wallpaper mechanism refinement**: because wallpaper absorbs some paint, the finished surface comes out slightly matte, hiding fingerprint/grease marks that show clearly on a directly-painted wall — a specific visual mechanism for this store's existing paintable-wallpaper-preference theme. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Wall-finish assignment (which covering on which wall) should be spelled out explicitly, wall-by-wall, in design-project documentation.** [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Laminated-finish window sills/slopes should match the window's laminate color, not default to white** — window manufacturers generally don't sell matching sill/slope panels, and matching-color panels are hard to find at typical building-materials markets; the fix is sourcing through specialized companies, not general retail. Repeatedly observed failure pattern: premium laminated windows paired with mismatched white sills/slopes. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Curtain cornices are now installed flush with, or slightly above, the ceiling plane** via a dedicated ceiling profile (previously hand-built from drywall), hiding mounting hardware above the curtain's top edge rather than leaving a cornice visibly hanging below the ceiling. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Second, upper electrical outlet inside a window slope**, in addition to a lower one, specifically for seasonal window/holiday light garlands — avoids visibly dangling cords from a low outlet; the upper outlet sits normally hidden behind closed curtains most of the year. Extends this store's existing window-slope-outlet content. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Quartz-vinyl (SPC) flooring used on nearly every project by this company, including budget tiers** — installs as one continuous field across room boundaries without transition strips at doorways; even a cheaper quartz-vinyl product line is stated as still water-resistant and seamless-installable, not just premium tiers. [source: yt_fx1vISZZK9s]
- **Kitchen curtain necessity depends on whole-apartment window-covering consistency** — if every other room uses roller blinds rather than curtains, a kitchen with no curtain zone designed into the layout doesn't need one either; not a universal "never curtain a kitchen" rule. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Large fixed panoramic window panes that cannot be manually washed should be cleaned with a magnetic window-cleaning gadget (manual or motorized)**, not industrial climbers (expensive, inconsistent quality) or by ordering the sash split into operable sections — splitting a large fixed facade pane into opening sections is described as tearing hinges/sagging over time, creating a cold thermal bridge that ices over in winter, and **triggering a facade-code violation with a formal restoration order plus fines**. Genuinely new panoramic-glazing maintenance + facade-modification regulatory-risk warning. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Always pre-size a radiator recess/niche for a concealment grille even for a client who currently wants an open radiator** — clients commonly change their mind after living with an exposed radiator, but retrofitting a niche later is often impossible once walls are finished; pre-sizing costs little extra at design time. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Standard multi-hinge revision/access hatches sag measurably over time** — real example: an even ~2mm perimeter gap widened to 4mm on one edge (0mm/binding on the opposite edge) within about a week of installation, worsening irreversibly once it starts. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Avoid automatic/sensor-triggered fixtures (motion-activated exhaust fans, automatic lighting) in favor of simple manual on/off switching** — automatic triggers are described as uncontrollable, acting on their own sensor logic rather than user intent. `single-account` design philosophy. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Study/homework desk placement rule**: should sit strictly to the right of a window so daylight falls from the left onto the writing hand (classroom-layout model). A general work desk has more placement freedom but should never face directly into a window — repeated pupil adjustment between a bright window and a screen is cited as accelerating vision strain. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Comfortable desk width is 70-80cm; a 60cm desk works only for a small laptop**, becoming unworkable once a monitor (demonstrated at 21") is added, since required viewing distance forces an awkward, back-fatiguing elbow/keyboard position. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Long-bedroom wardrobe placement hack**: shift the doorway opening up to ~80cm from its classic position to free space for a deeper (60-70cm) wardrobe, with the door swinging directly onto the wardrobe face — the usual floor door-stop is replaced with a small adhesive door-stop sticker mounted on the wardrobe front. Only applicable when the bedroom has enough length to spare. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Telescoping wall-mounted drying racks as a space-efficient alternative to a fixed ceiling rack** — folds to a minimal footprint but extends to hang a full washing-machine load; multiple units (2-3) can be installed in the same small room at different heights when space is tight. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **TV electrical-point placement, two-part rule**: main TV power outlet goes directly behind the TV (not the lower/under-stand outlets meant for peripherals); for wall-mounted/bracketed TVs, the outlet must avoid the bracket's own footprint and not extend beyond the TV's visible frame — check typical bracket dimensions for the specified TV size before placing the point. Extends this store's existing TV-cable-provisioning content. [source: yt_ZX_J9uAXfeQ]
- **Floating/wall-hung furniture and wall-mounted TVs need reinforced wall backing planned at design stage, never decided at the end of a renovation** — ordinary wall attachment risks the furniture/TV detaching under normal use. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **TV signal cable channel must never be cut into a load-bearing wall**: the channel needs a minimum 60mm groove depth, but load-bearing-wall rebar typically sits at only 40mm depth, risking striking structural reinforcement. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Duty/night light placement differs by room type**: low near the floor in general living spaces (avoids underfoot obstacles, eases eye adjustment before the main light); high in a WC specifically (usually nowhere suitable to mount low; needs enough brightness for accurate nighttime use without the harsh main light). [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Radiator wall-recessing depth rule**: niche must be at least 15cm deep, recessed directly into a soft exterior wall or furred out on a load-bearing one; if the wall is too thin for a full 15cm recess, combine both approaches (e.g. 10cm recessed + 5cm furred) to reach the minimum. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Back-to-back wardrobe-niche technique**: split one dividing wall between two rooms into offset segments to create a mirrored recessed niche on each side; niche depth must match purpose — minimum 50cm for a technical/utility closet, minimum 60cm for an outerwear closet. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Quartz-vinyl (SPC) flooring installation-cost distinction**: locking installation is standard labor with no separate charge; glue-down installation is a specialized install billed as an extra line item. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Reinforced-edge interior doors cost the same as standard doors but last longer** — a metal edge profile replaces the standard laminate edge strip that chips at corners over time, at no price premium. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Mirrored door insert panels are one of the worst common design choices**: frequently create the illusion the door is ajar (especially unsettling in a WC), show fingerprints immediately, and increase door cost. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Opening/door-casing height-matching rule**: all openings trimmed with door-casing-extension material must match the top-casing height of the interior doors themselves, or a visible 3-4cm mismatch results between adjacent openings. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Built-in wardrobes are strictly better than framed/corpus wardrobes in cost, space, speed, and repairability — but designers reportedly favor corpus wardrobes anyway because furniture companies pay designers a kickback on corpus sales, with no commission on a direct client-to-manufacturer built-in purchase.** `single-account`, a candid disclosure of a structural industry conflict of interest — directly relevant to this project's self-managed delivery-model preference. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Built-in wardrobe base/plinth must be built taller than the standard baseboard height**, or the baseboard running up to the wardrobe door will physically block the door from opening. Even experienced furniture installers reportedly forget this since neither doors nor baseboards are typically installed yet at measurement time. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Walk-in closet requires a dedicated exhaust fan tied into the building's shared ventilation with a mandatory check-valve**, or a strong kitchen exhaust fan elsewhere in the unit can pull cooking odors backward into stored clothing. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Sofa-bed selection rule**: a split-mattress-cushion design should always be chosen over a single continuous-mattress design, at only a marginal price difference — faster/easier to fold, and noticeably more comfortable (a continuous mattress creates a gap/trough between cushions when unfolded). [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Lift-mechanism storage bed costs roughly 10,000-15,000 RUB more than a standard bed**, with recovered storage volume roughly comparable to an average wardrobe's capacity. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Avoid light fixtures that cycle color-temperature presets by click order (warm/cold/neutral)** — independently corroborates this batch's existing anti-recommendation from `yt_O_Gp1FPzkqQ` (same channel, separate video): clients reportedly love these fixtures for about a month before the color mismatch between adjacent fixtures becomes a constant irritant. [source: yt_K-W4dLJGEtg]
- **Self-managed/unsupervised renovations reportedly run ~1.5-2x higher on material costs than client-supervised ones**, per the practitioner's own stated experience — a direct argument for active owner oversight, independent of whether a company or a self-assembled crew executes the work. `single-account`, `unverified` as an industry statistic. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Entrance-door ergonomic-clearance standard**: layout should let a person enter without squeezing past the opening door or being struck by it from behind — demonstrated against a real bad neighboring-apartment example (~10cm clearance) vs. adequate clearance for a stroller/bicycle. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Full-length mirror on the entrance door itself, only as a fallback when wall space is under 40cm wide** — a compromise solution (gets smudged near the door handle), not a first choice. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Long-distance stretch-ceiling-to-wardrobe attachment boxes phased out in favor of bringing furniture flush to the ceiling with a dedicated top module** — reason: furniture installers can't reliably match the company's own precise box tolerances, leaving a visible gap/color-mismatch strip. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **In-wardrobe "home server" cabinets phased out in favor of a separate dedicated low-voltage equipment cabinet** — dust accumulation and accidental cable damage during cleaning were recurring client pain points; the standalone cabinet holds less but is judged the net-better trade-off. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Tier-specific wall-finish compatibility rule**: comfort-class renovations use only paintable wallpaper; business-class avoids paintable wallpaper because concealed door frames/baseboards (a business-class-tier feature) pair only with painted walls — a wallpaper edge meeting a concealed frame visibly frays over time. Genuinely new rule resolving an apparent tension in this store's existing wall-finish content. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Recessed (false-wall) radiators paired with ceiling-recessed curtain cornices cost no usable floor space**, contrary to client intuition — curtains occupy a fixed plane regardless of whether the radiator behind it is recessed, since curtains can never overlap the radiator's own zone either way; recessing is therefore "free" relative to the curtain footprint, while also eliminating a dust-collecting niche and visible pipework. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Kitchen-living (or any heavily-used large kitchen) AC is treated as a strict planning requirement** — the practitioner states every project without it eventually retrofits AC later at much higher cost (wall-channel cutting, full wallpaper replacement); if budget doesn't allow immediate install, pre-installed capped conduit stubs should be left ready for later. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Switch-to-fixture nearest-switch mapping rule, company-tested**: in a room with multiple switches/fixture groups, the physically nearest switch should control the nearest fixture, not a farther/diagonal one — the company states it validated this on several hundred people, since people instinctively reach for the nearest switch when leaving a zone. Large unlabeled multi-gang ("piano") switch panels should be avoided in comfort-class work, reserved for business-class-and-above (200,000-500,000+ RUB/m² total project cost). [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Split/divided galley-kitchen layout technique for an awkward corner-constrained space**: split into two connected runs (e.g. a straight run with bar counter + a shorter auxiliary run) joined by a single step, rather than a small useless corner or one overly long run consuming the whole living-room footprint. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Concealed-thermostat hygienic-shower/bidet-sprayer systems require a specific pressure-relief-capable holder** — a standard holder (meant for a normal mixer) reliably causes a leak within roughly one to two weeks, since it doesn't relieve residual line pressure the way the correct holder type does. Extends this batch's existing hygienic-shower holder-shutoff content. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Flush bathroom-floor waterproofing/soundproofing standard**: bathroom floor built level with the rest of the apartment, made code-compliant via full apartment-wide waterproofing rather than a raised curb; combined soundproofing+waterproofing material systems let the whole floor act as a single leak-containing "basin." Paired with a leak-detection sensor system standard on every project. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Concealed hinge price point: ~3,000 RUB vs. ~700 RUB for a standard visible hinge (~4x)** — framed as a negligible difference against total renovation budget; recommend delaying door installation rather than cheaping out on hinge type, except in genuinely classical-style interiors where visible hinges are a deliberate aesthetic choice. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Concealed/wall-mounted mixer and thermostatic bathroom fixture systems: ~70,000 RUB (standard exposed set) vs. ~280,000 RUB (concealed system), roughly 4x** — explicitly not recommended for comfort-class given the price gap; recommended for business-class. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]
- **Modern high-power cooktops typically ship without a plug (bare hardwired terminal block)** — the supply cable run must be planned with sufficient length to reach the terminal block, itemized in design documentation with exact floor-height/reference-point coordinates so any qualified electrician (or a self-managed DIY client) can execute it without the original design firm. [source: yt_vExbp48IDiI]

### Kitchen / Plumbing / Electrical / Doors-Trim / Materials / Furniture — Apartment Defect Walkthrough (Category 5 trial, added 2026-08-17)

Dense, almost entirely non-promotional single-apartment defect walkthrough — see [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_MNVBzis94Yw_zems_b305_apartment_defect_walkthrough_40mln|extraction note]] for full detail. `single-account` throughout (one practitioner's on-camera critique of unnamed third-party work). No pricing content. [source: yt_MNVBzis94Yw]

- **Tall pull-out kitchen cabinet organizers reachable only via a 2-3-motion opening sequence** — photogenic for a kitchen photoshoot, an active daily-use annoyance ("great for photos, will infuriate you by the second real use").
- **Kitchen electrical outlet mounted too high above the countertop, physically colliding with the countertop edge and blocking plug insertion** — the inverse failure mode to this store's existing 110cm-outlet-height/too-low-clearance mechanism (`yt_WVfgE0lHgVc`), confirming outlet-to-countertop clearance is error-prone in both directions.
- **Integrated/flush sink cutout still needs adequate adjacent counter width** — the cutout style alone (praised generally as one of renovation's best solutions) doesn't excuse an undersized counter strip beside it; items placed on too-narrow a strip slide directly into the sink.
- **Faucet spout reach must be matched to actual counter/sink geometry, and a leak sensor should never sit in a faucet's splash path** — an over-long spout on a narrow counter section splashed onto an adjacent leak sensor, triggering a false leak alarm nearly every morning.
- **Decorative pendant lighting is not a reliable sole primary light source — test it alone, not combined with other fixtures still on**, to judge real adequacy. Extends this store's existing kitchen mandatory-task-lighting rule (`yt_WVfgE0lHgVc`) to a second failure mode (insufficient *general* light, not just missing task light).
- **Decorative wall panel bays should be planned together with electrical-point placement**, not sequentially — a panel installed without centering outlets/switches within each bay produces a visible clash.
- **Water-supply shutoff/distribution node placed inside an entryway coat closet is a poor room/function pairing**, even when the leak-protection sensor system on that same node is good practice independent of the placement choice.
- **Extractor-fan switch for a bathroom/WC must be located inside or immediately adjacent to the room it serves, never remote** — second independent corroboration (alongside `yt_s5Gyi8Rd9D0`) of this store's existing bathroom-switch-usability rule.
- **Multi-scene "piano" light-switch panels are frequently under-used relative to their wiring cost, per this practitioner's stated experience** (`single-account`, explicitly flagged as one practitioner's opinion, not corroborated elsewhere in this store) — demonstrated real-world consequence: took ~30 minutes on camera to map an installed panel, with roughly half its switches non-functional/unassigned.
- **Vacuum-cleaner-dedicated outlets spaced much closer than a standard cord's reach (here, ~1.5m intervals) are over-provisioned** relative to genuine need.
- **Two visually identical doors in the same unit should swing the same direction** — installing twins to swing differently is a confusing, avoidable inconsistency.
- **A deep built-in storage recess (here, ~75cm, sized for a full-depth washer) needs symmetric trim/reveal widths planned on both sides in advance** — a real example had mismatched 5cm/15cm reveals from not planning this up front.
- **Laminate flooring (a floating-floor product) must never be used as ceiling decorative cladding** — not rated for adhesive/overhead mounting, expands/contracts with humidity, and risks panels detaching and falling if the unit is ever flooded from above even lightly (a stated safety concern, not just cosmetic); demonstrated already visibly detaching on camera.
- **Paint-finish (non-wallpaper) walls along an exterior facade wall specifically are prone to cracking**, attributed to that wall's larger temperature/humidity swings — a recurring claim from this presenter across multiple sources in this store, `single-account`/repeated-channel-opinion, not yet independently corroborated by a non-Zemstandart source.
- **Closet clothing-rod height must be set for the actual household's height range, not a single "tall" default** — a rod set for a tall occupant left average/shorter household members unable to reach it even jumping.
- **A kept "space-saving" developer niche with a non-standard width is not real space savings if it fits no standard furniture size** — either wall it off entirely or hand the full room dimension back; don't leave an ambiguous in-between gap.
- **Bedside clearance under ~15cm per side (short of a working ~30cm-per-side target) is a fall-risk-relevant placement/sizing error**, not just a tight-but-workable outcome — compounds from bed width, nightstand placement, and electrical-point calculation together.

### Planning Rules / Design Process — Layout Critique Against Client Brief + Redesign Methodology (Category 5 trial, added 2026-08-17)

Two-half video: written-brief-vs-delivered-plan critique, then the presenter's own redesign (second half ends in an explicit self-promotional CTA — technique kept, framing flagged per the Advertising/Promotional Content Notes section above). See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_cidd4YHBJdA_zems_b256_layout_critique_and_redesign_412500rub|extraction note]] for full detail, including the client-brief checklist and the specific delivered-plan failures found against it. No pricing content beyond the Design Project Fees table entries above. [source: yt_cidd4YHBJdA]

- **Verify a delivered floor plan against the client's own written technical brief item by item, not just for general design competence** — the video's structural device, and a genuinely reusable planning-verification method independent of this channel: read the full stated brief first, then check the delivered plan against each item individually rather than judging it holistically.
- **A workspace desk should not default to facing directly into a window** — cited eyesight-strain risk from backlighting/glare absent corrective lighting design. Complements this store's existing rule that a child's desk shouldn't default to the window's *left* side specifically (hand-shadow mechanism, `web_zemspro_about_development`) and its existing desk/window eyesight-protection content (`yt_UoqC7xSFZ4Q`) — together these are now three independent desk-vs-window placement cautions with different underlying mechanisms (backlighting glare, hand-shadow, and the `yt_UoqC7xSFZ4Q` mechanism).
- **Wet-zone (санузел) footprint must be fixed and confirmed before any other layout step**, with all wet-zone plumbing kept within that boundary — in this project the client's own residential complex reportedly fines and forces reversal on residents who expand a wet zone beyond its original footprint. `single-account`, level-2 regulatory claim tied to one named residential complex, not independently verified — does not clear this store's stricter bar for the separate regulations store.
- **Door-opening layout allowance, general planning rule**: 1000mm setback from the intersecting wall face = 100mm trim standoff + minimum 800mm clear opening width + 100mm second standoff. `confirmed`, demonstrated twice in the same redesign for different openings — distinct from (and more general than) this store's existing foam-block-specific 78cm clear-width figure (`yt_OR0Vk7V6zeo`).
- **Wall-thickness convention: 150mm for a new partition specifically needing sound isolation between two rooms (e.g. a bedroom/kids-room divide); 100mm for all other new partitions**, to minimize lost floor area.
- **TV-niche sizing must use the TV's actual physical measurements, not a diagonal-to-dimension conversion chart alone** — such charts ignore the bezel/body depth; a real client's TV didn't fit a niche sized purely off the chart.
- **Under central (not autonomous) heating, a balcony-block's radiator position cannot be relocated as part of "mirroring" the balcony door/window layout** — a fixed-branch heating riser feeding that radiator would need pipe run under the screed to a new position, degrading that radiator's performance; the only adjustable element under central heating is shifting the door opening left/right within the same wall run. First explicit statement of this central-vs-autonomous-heating distinction in this store's existing balcony-mirroring content.
- **A loggia/balcony sitting above an unheated space below nearly always needs a raised floor/podium** when incorporated into heated living space, to get proper insulation — extends this store's existing loggia-insulation content with the specific "unheated space below" mechanism.
- **Loggia-based desk/work-surface ergonomic height target: 75cm from the loggia's own (raised) floor plane.** `single-account`.
- **Merging a loggia into a room requires false-wall returns to compensate for removing the window/balcony-block's structural mounting ledge ("quarter")**, keeping the resulting wall plane flat and continuous. `single-account`, technical detail not yet independently corroborated elsewhere in this store.
- **A chamfered/sloped shelf-edge near a frequently-used doorway helps prevent collision injury** — complements this store's existing flush-mounting collision-avoidance rule (`yt_s5Gyi8Rd9D0`) with a second, distinct technique (chamfered edge vs. flush mounting) for the same underlying concern.
- **Size a storage niche's depth to its actual planned contents (here: document folders/binders)**, not a default — extends this store's existing depth-by-purpose closet framework (`yt_VgsPDOcPV7c`, `yt_WVfgE0lHgVc`) to a documents-storage use case.

### Electrical / Doors-Trim / Flooring / WC / Furniture — "TOP 15 Stupidest Mistakes" Dense Round-Up (Category 3 batch, added 2026-08-18)

Single-apartment defect walkthrough, part 1 of a multi-part series. `single-account` throughout. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_8QWuVV_qmx0_top15_stupidest_mistakes_292|extraction note]] for full detail. [source: yt_8QWuVV_qmx0]

- **Electrical panel height: ~140cm from floor**, so even a shorter-than-average household member can operate it in an emergency.
- **Entry light-switch placement: ~90cm from floor, ~15cm from the opening edge** (95cm if the household is tall) — found "blind" on entering/leaving a room. **Named explicitly "the Zems standard" by a same-batch companion source** (`yt_wFUUakbL5O8`, below) — treat as this channel's stated standing convention.
- **Windowsill overhang: 40mm** — too little wastes usable width, too much risks cracking/bending, worse on a glossy finish.
- **Entrance-door casing material should match the interior doors' own casing/jamb-extension material family**, even though the two door classes differ.
- **Glossy windowsill finish on a loggia is a poor choice** — street dirt, installation waviness, and scratches all show worse on gloss than matte.
- **Never use drywall boxing to fake a load-bearing element's geometry or to avoid straightening an irregular wall/ceiling.**
- **Baseboard style must be consistent within a single room** — never mix multiple baseboard treatments in one space.
- **A loggia countertop meant as a real workspace must be built at proper desk height**, not left atop the developer's low windowsill height.
- **Decorative niches are dust/dirt traps that are hard to clean**, worse with real-wood trim; avoid narrowing an already-tight doorway opening with one.
- **Entrance-door casing needs a minimum ~3cm standoff/reveal from the wall on both sides.**
- **A "dirty zone" near an entrance must be a genuine physically-distinct zone** (different flooring material/color), not merely implied by a decorative element like a mismatched tile plus a mirror.
- **Attached/merged-loggia flooring needs a raised podium**, with its threshold/step built flush to the vent-grille plane, not an awkward two-step ledge.
- **Wall-hung toilets are preferred over floor-mounted, especially in a small WC** — less floor footprint, easier to clean around, height-adjustable at install.
- **Decorative faux ceiling beams/battens in an entryway are a poor choice**: outdated, expensive despite being non-structural, hard to clean in corners, and block furniture placement against that wall.
- **A "massage"-type shower head (scalp-contact function) must be mounted low enough to actually reach the head.**
- **Avoid unnecessary new exterior/convex wall corners in a layout** — they reliably take impact damage over normal use.

### Electrical / Plumbing / Flooring / HVAC / Furniture — "90 Regrets After Designer Renovation" (Category 3 batch, added 2026-08-18)

Single-apartment defect walkthrough with several numeric standards independently corroborating the round-up above within this same batch. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_wFUUakbL5O8_90_regrets_designer_renovation_233|extraction note]] for full detail. [source: yt_wFUUakbL5O8]

- **"Zems standard" switch height (90cm/15cm-from-opening, 95cm if tall) and the 40mm windowsill-overhang figure are independently restated in this source** — two same-channel data points now corroborating each other within one batch (still single-channel, not cross-channel).
- **Extractor-fan switch for a bathroom must be inside or immediately adjacent to the room it serves** — third independent corroboration in this store (alongside `yt_s5Gyi8Rd9D0` and `yt_MNVBzis94Yw`).
- **Bedroom bedside main-light switch must be reachable from both sides of the bed** — corroborates/extends this store's existing two-way bedroom-switch rule (`yt_WVfgE0lHgVc`).
- **Countertop must be cut to reach fully to the wall, with a parallel cut** — no unreachable dead space behind the run.
- **Flooring should run as one continuous single-color surface**, no "designer insert" color-break strip or open threshold between zones of the same open-plan room.
- **AC indoor-unit placement, exterior-restriction branch**: default to routing the condensate drain and outdoor condenser to the building exterior; **if the building/HOA prohibits any exterior condenser/condensate hardware** (real example: an "elite" new-build development), place the indoor unit above the entrance door instead, avoiding direct airflow onto a seating/sleeping occupant.
- **Prefer concealed curtain cornices and concealed (boxed-in) radiators over open/visible ones.**
- **Reserve genuine plumbing rough-in (water supply and drain, not just an outlet) if a spot is intended for a washing machine.**
- **A sink should not be positioned so close to a toilet that it pinches the seating/use area.**
- **Avoid boxing in drywall specifically to house one or two recessed spotlights in a bathroom ceiling** — prefer a backlit/"glowing" stretch-ceiling panel; corroborates this store's existing anti-recessed-spotlight preference (`yt_8odGOTVAuIk`).
- **Structural-column/infill-partition misalignment must always be plastered flush**, with a full cost cascade if skipped: extra material and labor to work the corner, extra baseboard-fitting labor, extra stretch-ceiling cost, and — most consequential — it forces custom, non-standard-dimension furniture since the resulting wall has no canonical size to fit against.

### Furniture / Built-ins — Kitchen Cabinet Assembly Masterclass, 28-Item DIY Checklist (Category 3 batch, added 2026-08-18)

Near-zero-promotion, pure DIY technique video — the densest, most directly self-managed-relevant source from this channel to date. Full 28-item checklist in [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_Y_Tuxcd-VxM_28_mistakes_lower_kitchen_modules_153|extraction note]] (unpacking/inspection, hardware sorting, torque setting, sequential-tightening order, floor protection, leg-offset marking, two-person handling, bracket-edge clearance, lift-don't-slide, runner-orientation/screw-length checks, L/R and top/middle panel-marking checks, back-panel fastening method and spacing, squareness-check-before-final-fastening, eccentric-fastener sequencing, tall-module leg-install order, no-back-panel-on-fridge-module rule, pre-drill-before-install for the countertop rail, keep-assembly-area-clear, corner-first installation order, level-before-tying by both height and depth, and hardware-after-countertop-cutouts sequencing). [source: yt_Y_Tuxcd-VxM]

### Planning Rules / Project Management — Client Testimonial, Third-Party Contractor Experience (Category 3 batch, added 2026-08-18, partial extraction)

Title-misleading source ("30 defects" implies a checklist; actual content is a client-testimonial interview, heavily self-promotional). Only genuinely reusable lessons extracted — see [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_0As5ICyk-jU_30_defects_30_sqm_client_testimonial|extraction note]] for the excluded narrative. No pricing content resolvable (only relative comparisons, no absolute figures). [source: yt_0As5ICyk-jU]

- **If hiring a separate execution crew from the one that produced the design documentation, the client must actively verify the crew is following the drawings** — a real project's crew struggled to read/follow written documentation, requiring repeated client site visits to catch deviations.
- **Always over-provision two-way ("crossover") switch-wiring circuits during rough electrical** — a real project under-wired these, discovered only at finish stage, requiring channels re-cut into already-plastered walls.
- **A worksite left unfinished/exposed for an extended pause (real example: ~1.5 months) risks real material degradation**: drywall-joint seams separating, plaster falling off box-outs, and — most seriously — a ceiling section collapsing during later demolition because it had been fixed only to since-removed wall framing rather than the structural ceiling.
- **Loggia insulation thickness must be planned before window placement is finalized** — a real project needed a full teardown and window repositioning after under-building the insulation buildup.
- **A wrong-gauge circuit-cable replacement is a real, hard-to-diagnose failure mode** — illustrates the value of an independent electrical inspection when a diagnosis stalls, and the risk of a casually-executed circuit-cable re-pull (real example: neutral and ground crossed between two different cables during a gauge upgrade, ~1 month to diagnose).

### Bathroom-WC / Kitchen / Walls-Ceilings / Plumbing / Windows-Slopes — "7 Key Life Hacks" Universal Technique Batch (Category 3 batch, added 2026-08-18)

Explicitly framed by the presenter as a departure from the usual critique format — zero promotional content, universal brand-agnostic tips. ASR auto-generated captions (some garbled passages, not relied on). See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_scOLgA4HPqM_7_key_lifehacks_099|extraction note]] for full detail. [source: yt_scOLgA4HPqM]

- **Bathtub-recess resizing rule**: narrow an oversized wet-zone footprint to one of three standard tub-recess widths — 1.60m (too small), **1.70m (this practitioner's stated optimum)**, or 1.80m (for tall occupants).
- **Tub-length comfort mechanism**: a too-long tub leaves a shorter occupant without foot purchase against the far end, causing them to slide toward the drain while reclining.
- **Never leave a flat tiled horizontal ledge/shoulder around an undersized tub set into an oversized recess** — moisture/grime collect and grout deteriorates; the tub must match its recess so tile overlaps the rim directly. Reinforces this store's existing tile-overhangs-tub-rim rule.
- **Kitchen electrical rough-in sequencing, when the manufacturer isn't chosen yet**: form rough geometry → bring in that company's own measurer once selected → sign the contract → get written technical requirements for exact electrical/water-drain positions → only then finalize rough-in. No universal kitchen-company standard exists across manufacturers.
- **Never add extra insulation or a heat-reflective panel behind a radiator niche recessed into an exterior wall**, and don't thin the wall further there. **Mechanism**: a wall's interior/exterior temperature gradient equalizes roughly at its midpoint; insulating/reflecting behind the niche blocks the room's own warm air from reaching and warming that thinned section from inside — colder/condensation-prone, the opposite of the intuitive fix. A wall thinned ~15cm by a radiator niche still stays warmer than most modern panel-building exterior walls without any special treatment.
- **Leak-protection shutoff-valve sequencing rule**: the electromechanical shutoff valve (paired with floor sensors) must sit immediately after each riser's first shutoff valve, before any other fitting — including a coarse pre-filter — so nothing downstream is left unprotected. One device per riser (2 for a standard single-riser-pair apartment). Extends this store's existing leak-sensor content with an explicit installation-order rule.
- **Always replace developer-supplied stock plumbing manifolds/collectors with new ones** — developer-installed fixtures are the cheapest available grade, per this practitioner's stated experience of near-universal 1-2-year leak/flood callbacks when skipped.
- **Insulate every rough cold/hot supply pipe run in foam sleeve, regardless of manufacturer**, to prevent surface condensation.
- **Widening a window/light opening on a non-load-bearing exterior wall is recommended by default wherever practical.** Hard limits: minimum 10cm return/pier on each side; minimum 20cm clearance to the ceiling/stretch-ceiling plane if extending upward.
- **When merging a loggia into a room, do not remove the low windowsill-height ledge/nook or relocate the radiator beneath the old window** — occupants consistently end up wanting a small ledge/surface there regardless; leaving it (built as a proper counter/shelf) avoids informal clutter re-accumulating while still gaining the light/opening benefit.

### Doors-Trim / Furniture / Materials / Plumbing / Mistakes-Warnings — "13 Life Hacks" Project Walkthrough (Category 3 batch, chunk 2, added 2026-08-18)

Direct walkthrough of the presenter's own ~95.5 m² project, zero critique/CTA. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_WBaKEl5HIzU_13_lifehacks_093|extraction note]] for full detail. [source: yt_WBaKEl5HIzU]

- **A remote-controlled (electromagnetic) entrance-door lock is a genuine security-vs-emergency-access tradeoff**: if its control electronics fail, the door cannot be forced open even by fully cutting the hinge side (passive deadbolt pins on the hinge side block that bypass). Explicit caution: think carefully before installing this hardware type.
- **Sliding-wardrobe top rail must be fastened into a dedicated backing block or thick plywood, never into drywall/profile framing alone** — repeated door-slam impact loosens a drywall-only fastener over time.
- **Handover documentation practice for a wardrobe box/soffit built before a stretch ceiling**: photograph it for the client, have the client independently photograph it too, and write a note directly on the box stating clearance-to-ceiling and a max-fastener-length caution — prevents a later installer puncturing the hidden ceiling membrane.
- **An electric tile cutter is mandatory for any 45° miter cuts** — manual/angle-grinder 45° cuts are frequently low quality.
- **Some building-management companies restrict independent riser-level plumbing adjustments** even for legitimate pressure/quality fixes — a real cited case where management refused DIY riser work and instead performed a lower-quality fix themselves.
- **Critical, safety-relevant radiator/laminate expansion-gap rule**: a rigid pipe penetrating laminate flooring needs the same expansion gap used around the room's perimeter (typically 1.5cm) applied around the pipe penetration too, covered with a decorative flange — cutting the penetration snug (as some manufacturer diagrams show) can let the floor's humidity/temperature expansion push against and shift or burst the rigidly-fixed pipe/radiator assembly, risking a flood. Genuinely important mechanism not previously recorded this specifically in this store.
- **Real historical (2019 Moscow) pricing data point**: paintable wallpaper for a ~95.5 m² apartment — standard grade ~32,000 RUB, premium grade actually used ~130,000 RUB. Not added to any comparison table (single data point, no comparable wallpaper-pricing series exists yet) — recorded for reference only, historical.

### Planning Rules / Layout Technique — "10 Life Hacks" + "20 Life Hacks" Project Walkthroughs (Category 3 batch, chunk 2, added 2026-08-18)

Two companion project walkthroughs from the same era (2019). See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_dV0OLE16wzg_10_lifehacks_084|note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_CDklNSpLB9g_20_lifehacks_080|note]] for full detail. [sources: yt_dV0OLE16wzg, yt_CDklNSpLB9g]

- **Never leave a partial-depth wall stub/jog** — demolish and rebuild flush; the cost is recovered by avoiding extra stretch-ceiling corners and an ugly baseboard/cornice detail.
- **A deliberately-kept narrow wall pier next to an opening can anchor a large adjacent storage build-out** — a narrower opening with more storage can beat a wider opening with less.
- **Wet-zone relocation is more likely approvable when the new location sits above a hallway/corridor in the unit below, not above another unit's living space.** `single-account`, level-2 regulatory-adjacent claim, not independently verified.
- **Consolidate cramped multi-way doorway junctions by redistributing entries through a wider adjoining space.**
- **Technique: convert an oversized room's excess area into storage by moving a dividing wall inward, leaving the vacated strip as a deep recessed closet.**
- **Technique: shorten a long, poorly-proportioned room (fixed length via a load-bearing end wall) by building a new partition partway in**, converting the "lost" end into a separate walk-in closet — resolves bad proportions and gains storage without losing core function.
- **Split one large developer entry hall into two zones** — enables per-zone flooring material choice and creates room for a large built-in wardrobe plus a fast-access changing area.
- **Widen a corridor adjacent to a bathroom/WC pair to eliminate door-swing collision risk.**
- **Renovation debris volume is roughly comparable between new-build and existing units** — the real difference is limited to demolition-debris volume, not overall job-site waste.
- **A moderate (not maximum-width) kitchen-living opening is sufficient** — leave a durable, solid pier section at the opening since occupants inevitably improvise it into an informal bar-counter/leaning surface.
- **Radiators don't always need a full false wall to be recessed** — sometimes recessing directly into the existing wall with minor plumbing-routing trim is sufficient and cheaper.
- **Reinforce (frame in) the wall section above any planned curtain-cornice mounting point in advance**, even if not installed immediately.
- **Size a decorative niche's depth to closely match its actual intended contents** — excess depth wastes plaster (real example: 5-6 bags beyond what was needed).
- **Split a long, awkward L-shaped/corner loggia into two zones with a dividing partition** when it has two separate exterior access doors; with one access point, still section off a portion as dedicated storage.
- **Verify a corner is genuinely 90° with a laser square before a built-in kitchen install** — cheap to fix during rough work, expensive to fix after cabinetry installation.
- **Plastering precision spec: max ~5mm deviation from a straightedge, ideally 1-3mm** — plaster costs roughly 2x base filler compound because reaching that tolerance often needs two full passes; flags the common bad practice of papering over sloppy plastering with heavy filler instead of plastering correctly the first time.
- **Provide (or contractually require) proper hot-water shower facilities for on-site crew living at the jobsite** — corroborates the temporary-shower-cabin practice in this batch's `yt_WBaKEl5HIzU`.

### Electrical / Furniture — "Best Electricity and Low-Current Life Hacks" + "Best Kitchen Solutions" (Category 3 batch, chunk 2, added 2026-08-18)

Two purely technical 2022 videos. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_rX8Ag60l-RA_best_electricity_lowcurrent_lifehacks_176|note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_MzEwow_CPR4_best_kitchen_solutions_175|note]] for full detail. [sources: yt_rX8Ag60l-RA, yt_MzEwow_CPR4]

- **Electrical/low-current-point centering must account for the finished door-casing width (typically 5-7cm), not the raw opening** — a very common mistake leaves points permanently off-center once casing is added.
- **Dedicated low-current distribution cabinet, separate from the main electrical panel** — a small covered cabinet (sized so a router's WiFi signal isn't degraded) instead of an exposed, dust-collecting cable tangle.
- **Combined electrical+low-current "technical column" technique**: dedicate one narrow closet section (~110mm deep) purely as a technical bay for both panels, rather than recessing into a non-structural wall or building an entire closet shallower.
- **P-44T-series ventilation-shaft-in-entry-hall fix**: extend the adjacent wall flush with the shaft's depth (min ~50cm) and box in a matching ceiling soffit, enabling a built-in (not free-standing) wardrobe — corroborates this store's existing built-in-vs-corpus cost/practicality rule with a specific application.
- **Kitchen island clearance/sizing rules, precise numbers**: minimum 80cm clearance on every side except one facing a sofa/dining area; minimum 1m island width; never split a 1m island into two back-to-back zones with no knee space — leave a **minimum 35cm (ideally 40cm) knee recess** for seated use.
- **Island height should match its actual intended use** (cooking-zone height matches the main counter; bar height determined by trying stool heights) — no universal correct island height.
- **Avoid countertop-embedded pop-up/retractable outlets** — food/liquid residue reportedly jams the extend mechanism; prefer a flush, static, separately-lidded housing.
- **Dishwasher-to-sink handedness rule**: for a right-handed dishwasher, place the dishwasher to the sink's left — the free hand needs the dishwasher on the same side as the working hand holds a plate near the sink. Always ask the household which hand they wash dishes with.
- **Upper kitchen cabinet modules are frequently never actually used functionally** — a legitimate cost-saving measure is making them decorative-only (blank fronts, no functional hardware) with no visual downside.
- **Cooking-zone task lighting must be switched from within the cooking zone itself**, not remotely or via sensor.
