- **Plan-documentation color-coding convention**: draw new drywall-on-frame construction in a different color/fill than original masonry on any project plan, so a crew/inspector/client can immediately distinguish structure from added framing — corroborated 4 times across this cluster. [sources: W9cCxVQVVes, 1pU60p0Jh3A, and others in this cluster]
- **Hygiene-driven path-elongation technique**: deliberately route the entrance-to-kitchen walking path *longer* than the shortest available option, to put more distance between the dirty-shoe zone and food-prep areas. [source: HVSZh0lH9hk]
- **Kitchen module-width discipline**: never widen a cabinet module beyond an adjacent fixed-width appliance's own width (e.g. keep a storage module at 60cm next to a 60cm fridge) — an oversized module next to a fixed appliance reads as visually disproportionate. **Minimum comfortable linear-kitchen width: ~2.30m** below which no layout reconfiguration fixes the cramped result. [sources: HVSZh0lH9hk, ZVoExA0t6nI]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — First Batch (4 videos, added 2026-08-19)

**New candidate pool re-derived** by cross-referencing the full `_Inbox/transcripts/channel_fresh_titles.txt` channel manifest (158 rows) against every `watch?v=` URL in `00_Master/processed_sources.csv` — found 16 IDs never covered by any prior triage pass (Category 4, Category 5, or the Round 1 remainder-pool list). See `_Inbox/planning/zemskov_remainder_pool_20260819.md`'s "Round 2" table for the full list. This first batch of 4 (`DEs8V-mHHDo` #169, `C4lUAfJyyb0` #170, `Qw2Xi6uPLls` #302, `MWDcYHqe-iQ` #288) all fetched cleanly and cleared the value-filter bar. Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_DEs8V-mHHDo_kitchen_7_mistakes_169|DEs8V-mHHDo]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_C4lUAfJyyb0_developer_stolen_meters_170|C4lUAfJyyb0]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_Qw2Xi6uPLls_floating_dimension_drafting_technique_302|Qw2Xi6uPLls]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_MWDcYHqe-iQ_designer_apartment_walkthrough_288|MWDcYHqe-iQ]].

- **⚠️⚠️ Standout finding — the "floating dimension" construction-drafting technique**: on any construction drawing with a chain of dimensioned segments, specify only the dimensions that are genuinely functionally critical, and deliberately leave one non-critical segment's width unlabeled. A crew builds outward from the specified anchor points; since cumulative construction/plastering tolerance error has nowhere labeled to go, it automatically lands in the unlabeled ("floating") segment instead of eating into a dimension that must stay firm (worked example: a wardrobe's depth held to a firm 600mm by specifying only "600mm offset from a corner" and "150mm wall thickness" on one sheet, plus "500mm reveal" and "800mm opening" on a separate openings sheet — leaving one reveal deliberately unlabeled). **A general, broadly reusable construction-documentation principle**, not specific to any one room. [source: Qw2Xi6uPLls]
- **⚠️ Electrical-panel recess sizing formula**: minimum 110mm panel installation depth → build the niche to at least 140mm deep; if the host wall isn't thick enough to net a sound remainder after recessing, thicken it first (worked example: 200mm → 250mm). Full coordinate sequence: 1200mm up from the floor screed for the niche bottom, +500mm for niche height; 100mm in from the neighbor wall, +400mm for niche width. [source: C4lUAfJyyb0]
- **⚠️ Sequencing rule: model a wall's soundproofing-layer thickness before positioning any door opening near that wall** — getting this backwards forces the door casing to be notched/trimmed later to fit around the added layer, which looks worse than an intentionally asymmetric opening reveal chosen from the start (worked example: 115mm/185mm asymmetric reveal, driven by a 70mm acoustic layer — a second data point for this layer thickness, distinct from the more commonly-seen 50mm figure). [source: C4lUAfJyyb0]
- **Kitchen QC checklist, 7 points**: 15-20cm curtain-clearance gap between a kitchen run's end and a side wall; exactly 600mm countertop-to-upper-cabinet clearance for a clean 60cm-tile backsplash fit (with a fix technique if built wrong); a light-control switch must be reachable from within the actual cook zone, not tucked in a hard-to-reach corner; verify appliance door-swing/control-panel side before corner placement; floor-to-ceiling upper cabinets cost up to 1.5× more and the top tier needs a step stool regardless of height; a deep storage column needs a pull-out drawer system, not a simple hinged door, to use its full depth; extend upper-cabinet depth to a full 600mm around a corner-adjacent fridge rather than leaving a shallow, dust-collecting shelf above it. [source: DEs8V-mHHDo]
- **⚠️ "Appliance parking zone" (паркинг техники) clarified**: it's an extension of the main countertop at the *same height*, for appliances used *infrequently* — not any recessed/sunken nook. A documented misuse (a sunken nook mislabeled as a parking zone, functioning as neither a real parking zone nor a usable prep surface, plus creating an unnecessary sharp corner) sharpens this store's existing concept. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Kitchen cook-zone continuity rule**: when a fixed obstruction (e.g. a vent shaft) sits within a kitchen run, push the whole run outward past it rather than splitting the cook zone into two disconnected halves around it — splitting doesn't gain space, it breaks usability and creates an unnecessary sharp corner. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Door casing/baseboard color-and-material-consistency rule**: casings and baseboards should be the same color, ideally the same manufacturer/material, reading as one continuous trim element — never try to paint a door to match an adjacent wood-textured surface's exact color (use contrast instead). **Flooring-to-door-frame transition**: meet the frame directly with a small color-matched-sealant gap, never a separate visible threshold strip with exposed fasteners. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Master-switch differentiation rule**: a "master" switch that cuts power to the whole unit must sit at a different height/position and look visually distinct from the regular switch group, to prevent accidental presses. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Baseboard-integrated electrical distribution caution**: routing outlets/TV coax/network cabling through a baseboard channel system (rather than fixed wall points at furniture height) creates a furniture-placement conflict — plan electrical/data points at their intended furniture-height locations instead. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **⚠️ Combined wall-thickening technique**: thicken a wall by ~10cm to shift a bathtub's outer edge forward, simultaneously eliminating an awkward hard-to-clean ledge next to the tub *and* improving a wall-mounted mixer's reach from within the tub. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]
- **Bedroom door-reveal correction: 10cm, not 30cm**, on the side pushed toward for maximum adjacent-closet depth. **Decorative backlighting over a headboard**: striking for rental-listing photos, genuinely uncomfortable for daily use — independently corroborates the segment-tier-dependent design-choice theme first seen in `D8t1ADisUE8`. [source: MWDcYHqe-iQ]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — Second Batch (4 videos, added 2026-08-19)

Continuing Round 2's 16-video re-derived candidate list. Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_8mffcO5q-k_microwave_coffee_machine_slot_191|_8mffcO5q-k]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_OnKreLhmLYY_90_mistakes_part3_299|OnKreLhmLYY]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_nbrDFET2AXk_90_mistakes_part2_295|nbrDFET2AXk]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_vRLdOS5HTTU_got_back_stolen_space_298|vRLdOS5HTTU]]. **⚠️ Same-project note**: `OnKreLhmLYY` (#299) and `nbrDFET2AXk` (#295) are parts 3 and 2 of the same "90 mistakes" critique series about one single apartment — their overlapping findings (extreme switch count, a drywall-ceiling chemical-exposure claim) are the same practitioner re-narrating the same real defects, not independent corroboration; flagged in both source notes rather than double-counted here.

- **⚠️ Appliance tower-slot assignment should be driven by usage *frequency* and *maintenance/refill* action, not just each appliance's own "ideal ergonomic height" in isolation** — real client self-correction case: a coffee machine used multiple times daily was moved to the easily-reached lower tower slot (swapping with a rarely-used microwave), because the upper slot's bean-hopper refill point sat at ~1.75m, requiring an unreachable ~60cm-above-the-machine reach for a task performed several times a day. A household with staff to handle refills wouldn't hit this problem — the client's own hands-on refill routine is what surfaced it. [source: _8mffcO5q-k]
- **⚠️⚠️ Major finding: "improved"/denser door weatherstripping can break a magnetic door lock, and doesn't actually improve soundproofing anyway** — the door bounces off a denser seal before the magnetic latch engages, and the real sound-leak path (the gap under the door) isn't addressed by a denser perimeter seal at all. Don't pair a magnetic lock with "upgraded" weatherstripping without verifying compatibility. [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ Walk-in-closet-style open storage vs. simple wardrobe: a real case quantifies ~2.5× more floor area consumed for the *same* clothing capacity**, plus an exposed unprotected external corner — a concrete data point for this store's existing wardrobe-vs-walk-in tradeoff content (`14_Furniture/analysis/Wardrobe_vs_Walkin_Tradeoff.md`). [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ Textured wall finish + raking-light fixture interaction**: a light fixture that casts side/raking light visually amplifies every irregularity in a textured wall finish, reading as poor workmanship even when the texture is intentional — pair texture with diffuse-light fixtures instead. **Diagonal tile-transition** needs a real justification — without one it's just extra cuts/waste. **Glossy windowsill in a kitchen**: fingerprints, installation imperfections, and appliance scratches (especially if outlets in the reveal invite appliance placement there) all show more on glossy than matte, and a matte-frame pairing looks worse than either alone. [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ "Butt-first design" anti-pattern, named explicitly**: building a symmetric/decorative wall composition *before* confirming where functionally-necessary furniture will go — once the furniture lands where actually needed, it can wreck the intended symmetry. Confirm functional furniture placement first, decorative compositions second, never the reverse. [source: OnKreLhmLYY]
- **⚠️ Grid/lattice ceiling light fixture over a bed: avoid** — the grid blocks most of the light output *and* accumulates dust that continuously sheds onto the person below. **Bathtub-then-tile sequencing, stated definitively**: install the tub first flush against three walls, then tile *over* its rim — never tile first and push the tub against a finished perimeter. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Two-piece/nested decorative door handles develop noticeable play within ~1 week, worsening to real looseness by ~6 months** — noisy, uncomfortable, and the resulting mechanical edge can injure. Avoid decorative multi-piece handle assemblies for durability. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Duct-box/niche "fill vs. finish" decision heuristic**: a developer niche that isn't functionally needed is usually cheaper and more useful filled flat with lightweight masonry (~10cm aerated block) than finished as a feature — a flat filled wall also lets kitchen-module electrical points land without chasing an adjacent load-bearing wall. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Rendering skepticism, stated as general buyer guidance**: designers have an incentive to show "interesting geometry" in a rendering to justify their fee — complex features that look striking rendered often look worse built and cost significantly more; ask what a feature will actually look/cost like built, not just how it renders. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Wardrobe/closet-niche dimensional-consistency check**: verify a storage niche's width is uniform top-to-middle-to-bottom *before* choosing a door mechanism — a real ~4cm inconsistency made neither a sliding nor hinged system installable, forcing a downgrade to a shower-curtain-on-a-wire improvisation. [source: nbrDFET2AXk]
- **⚠️ Remote-client digitization workflow**: an accurate 3D model of an existing space can be built without an in-person site visit — a digitization specialist collects measurements remotely by phone/call, a project lead separately interviews the client to build the technical brief, then a technical department reviews/coordinates. A reusable process model for a design engagement that can't start with a site visit. **"Conflict color" plan-marking technique**: mark any wish-list item that doesn't fully fit a real layout in a distinct color during planning, rather than silently accepting a partial fit. [source: vRLdOS5HTTU]
- **Loggia rest-zone minimum width: ~1.10m, ideally 1.20m**, after glazing/insulation buildup — distinct from this store's general loggia-width content, specifically for a seating/lounging use. A second data point for the loggia-insulation-removal-for-space-gain technique: 17cm of width recovered in this project (vs. ~10cm/side, ~1m² total in an earlier source). [source: vRLdOS5HTTU]

### Remainder-Pool Round 2 — Third Batch (2 extracted + 1 duplicate-skip + 1 partial, added 2026-08-19)

Continuing Round 2's re-derived candidate list. Full detail in each source's own extraction note: [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_pNhM-kKBy6A_knowledge_base_reveal_294|pNhM-kKBy6A]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_u3UuZN9LHg0_developer_gandon_irregular_geometry_268|u3UuZN9LHg0]], [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/knowledge/sources/YT_eezwcNG-1qI_designer_project_walkthrough_269|eezwcNG-1qI]]. **⚠️ Confirmed duplicate**: `mhE_5qlJ0KU` ("Изменил двушку" #293) turned out to be a re-edited remake of already-processed `C4lUAfJyyb0` (#170) — same 69m² Odintsovo case, identical electrical-panel-niche formula and every worked number, explicitly framed in-video as an editing-style experiment by a different video editor. Logged `duplicate_skipped`, not re-extracted. **⚠️ Named-individual content excluded**: `pNhM-kKBy6A` contains a legal-dispute segment naming Karen Karapetyan (a recurring named-dispute target on this channel, already flagged repeatedly across this project's Category 5 processing) — the personal callout is not reproduced; only generalizable content extracted.

- **⚠️ Bathtub/shower sizing explicitly tied to occupant height**: 1700×700mm bathtub for an "average height" client; shower minimum 1500mm (ideal 1600-1700mm) length, minimum 900mm width — more explicit height-linked figures than this store's existing general sizing content. **Washing-machine-under-sink-countertop height-sensitivity caution**: the resulting elevated sink height isn't suitable for every client height — verify against the actual occupant before committing. [source: pNhM-kKBy6A]
- **⚠️ Ventilation shaft vs. duct box, the underlying safety mechanism spelled out**: never chase (штробить) into a true shared ventilation shaft, even a thin section — debris can fall down the shaft into units below, and the responsible party is fined. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **⚠️ Third documented instance of the "future-flexible planning" technique family**: pre-install reinforced wall backing (закладные) for a Swedish/gymnastic wall ladder *together with* hidden electrical for a TV zone at the same wall location, letting the client choose or later swap between the two without redoing the wall. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **⚠️ Cross-traffic-through-a-seating-zone elimination**: when an opening's position forces foot traffic directly in front of a TV-viewing seating area, relocate the *opening* (not just the furniture) to route traffic around the seating zone entirely. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **Deliberate flush-alignment exception, a second concrete instance**: leaving a step between two wall segments specifically because a piece of furniture (a closet) needs that step as its own back wall — reinforces the ~10%-exception rule already in this store. [source: u3UuZN9LHg0]
- **⚠️ Precise switch-reachability formula: 90cm from the floor, 15cm from the opening's edge** — demonstrated as the figures needed to find a switch reliably without looking. **Window-reveal outlet clearance: 10-15cm from the windowsill's own plane**, not flush against it. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **⚠️ Bed-away-from-window reasoning made explicit**: cold air drafts off large glazing plus nearby radiators drying the air make a window-adjacent sleep zone genuinely uncomfortable — keep the bed set back, put a daylight-dependent zone (makeup vanity) near the window instead. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **⚠️ Hygienic-shower/bidet-spray handedness, mechanism explained**: for a right-handed person, TP holder stays on the dominant (right) side, but the hygienic-shower spray goes on the *opposite* (left) side — the dominant hand washes while the non-dominant hand holds the spray wand. A more mechanism-explicit refinement of this store's existing handedness guidance. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **⚠️ Multi-function combo-appliance cost caution**: an all-in-one unit can cost ~3× a single-function equivalent — buy separate appliances matched to actual usage instead of paying a premium for unused capability. **Plisse (pleated) shades specifically recommended for loggia glazing** — allows partial open/close combinations in one shade; this channel's standard loggia specification. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]
- **Real comparative pricing data point (2024, self-reported)**: a client paid a competitor 225,000 RUB for a design project (vs. this channel's own ~160,000 RUB estimate) plus ~25,000 RUB/m² construction (vs. this channel's own ~20,000 RUB/m², dated 2021) — record as a documented comparison, not a verified market benchmark. [source: eezwcNG-1qI]

## Numeric Data / Measurements

Evidence-level key used below: **(1) transcript text** → **(2) YouTube metadata** → **(3) contextual inference** → **(4) external/domain validation**. Each entry states which level(s) actually support it; nothing is promoted past what the evidence supports.

> **Price comparability rule (per explicit user guidance, 2026-08-10 — full rule in `renovation-knowledge-intake/SKILL.md`): a price figure has real comparative value only once it carries both location/market AND year.** Record both explicitly next to every price entry below, not just once in a source note. Never compare/average/flag-as-conflicting two prices without both references resolved for each side — a gap explained by inflation (time) or a different market (location) is not a contradiction. Where a year is uncertain, check it via `yt-dlp` metadata rather than leaving it assumed. **Backlog, not yet done**: many entries below predate this rule and don't carry an explicit year/location pairing at the entry level (relying instead on a source's own date/region noted once elsewhere) — a systematic audit to add both to every entry is a future pass, not retrofitted here.

- **"Rational" budget tier** — Original statement (transcript): "100–120 за квадратный метр". Interpreted value: 100–120 тыс. руб./м² (thousand Russian rubles per m²). Basis: the video's own YouTube description independently states "100–120 тыс. за м²" (level 2, not just level 1). Confidence: `confirmed` (level 2). Independent verification: none beyond the video's own metadata (level 4 not yet applied). [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- **General construction materials, ~100 m² renovation** — Original statement: "35-45" (вилка/range, no unit restated in that sentence). Interpreted value: 35–45 тыс. руб. (thousand rubles), currency RUB by level-2 video-wide confirmation. Basis: unit carried forward from the confirmed 100–120 тыс./м² convention established elsewhere in the same video (level 3, contextual inference only). Confidence: `inferred`. Independent verification: none. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- **Labor/service pricing tiers** — Original statement: "20", "30", "40", "60", "80" (referenced individually across the passage, no unit restated per instance). Interpreted value: тыс. руб./м², by analogy to the confirmed 100–120 figure. Basis: level 3 (contextual/consistency inference) only. Confidence: `inferred`. Independent verification: none — not cross-checked against other figures in the same video or against outside sources. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- **Worked budgeting example** — Original statement: client-stated funds "15" → reliable funds "~12" → design fee "~5.000" → remainder "~11500" → "50-60%" of remainder to labor + general materials. Interpreted value: figures in millions of rubles for the "15"/"12" scale, thousands for "5.000"/"11500" (scale shift between figures noted, not smoothed over). Basis: level 1 (the sequence and percentages are directly stated) for structure/percentages; level 3 (inferred) for the absolute scale/units. Confidence: sequence/percentages `confirmed` (level 1), absolute units `inferred` (level 3). Independent verification: none. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- **Surface-mounted vs. built-in shower system** — Original statement: device "~20-25" vs. built-in "~100" installed; total gap "~150" vs. naive device-only gap "~75". Interpreted value: thousand rubles throughout, by the same level-3 convention. Basis: level 1 for the relationship/ratio claimed by the speaker; level 3 for units. Confidence: `unverified` (single source, not corroborated by level 4 external validation, and not cross-checked arithmetically against other figures in this video). Independent verification: none. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- **7komnat.by Novaya Borovaya (Minsk) project total** — Original statement: four group totals given directly in speech ($2,700 / $17,500 / $7,000 / $42,800), summing to $70,000 for 52 m² (≈$1,346/m²). Basis: level 1 (directly spoken), cross-checked at level 4 by summing the source's own itemized sub-figures against these group totals (see full case study §2a) — Group 2 matched exactly, Group 1 required resolving a $1,700-vs-$1,750 design-fee discrepancy in favor of $1,750 (arithmetic + description agree), Group 4 was $100 off (within plausible rounding). Confidence: `confirmed` for the group totals and grand total; the resolved $1,750 sub-figure is `confirmed` by two independent corroborating signals, with $1,700 recorded as a conflicting single mention, not deleted. Independent verification: internal arithmetic cross-check performed (level 4) — first source in this store to receive this treatment. [source: yt_jAdRyL6KFxs]
- **Speaker-stated general 2026 Minsk market rate** — Original statement: "около 1.000 долларов за метр квадратный" typical, "иногда... чаще переваливает" (often more); "700-800 долларов" achievable with a simplified/budget approach, described as hard to hit "in today's reality." Basis: level 1, directly spoken, but this is a general market claim by a single company's founder, not this project's own (arithmetically verified) figures — the speaker explicitly separates his own project's $1,346/m² from this general guidance. Confidence: `unverified` at the market-wide level (single company's professional opinion, no independent/outside corroboration), though the person making the claim runs a 10+-year Minsk renovation company per the description. Independent verification: none beyond the source's own stated business experience. [source: yt_jAdRyL6KFxs]

### Electrical / Lighting (new topic area, added 2026-08-04)

- **Mounting heights (from finished floor, this channel's standard)**: switch 90 cm (95 cm for tall households); switch-to-door-edge offset 15 cm; bedside outlet 70 cm; bedside lamp switch 120 cm (headboard) or 70 cm (nightstand); over-bed pendant cable drop 100 cm; countertop outlet 110 cm (shifts with a non-standard countertop height). Window-slope and ceiling-referenced points use the slope/sill or ceiling as datum instead of the floor. `confirmed`, level 1, repeated across multiple videos from the same channel. [source: yt_uLJGsTfTj3A, yt_l_rjjPlPkRo, yt_VK8rCdChdbY, yt_12o621100MQ]
- **Cable gauge / breaker pairing**: 1.5 mm²/10A lighting, 2.5 mm²/16A sockets, 4 mm²/20A ovens, 6 mm²/32A cooktops. Breaker curve B for sockets, curve C for lighting/AC, curve D not recommended residentially. `confirmed`, level 1, internally consistent across two independent videos from this channel. [source: yt_JrClDJb8WTM, yt_ciXeqvKDKSI]
- **Ganged socket box on-center spacing**: 71 mm. **Behind-TV cable channel minimum size**: 40 mm (round or square). `confirmed`, level 1. [source: yt_XmeoZUiGwBM, yt_wWteDpfIso]
- **Temporary rough-in wiring gauge**: 2×2.5 mm² ungrounded, vs. permanent 3×1.5 mm² (lighting) / 3×2.5 mm² (sockets), both grounded. Minimum clearance ≥1 m between a temporary bulb socket and an outlet block. `confirmed`, level 1, corroborated across two videos from this channel. [source: yt_VT-zl1Xay4A, yt_957RF_DcjwA]
- **Lighting comfort target**: ≥500 lux for main/task lighting; color temperature ~4000K main/task, ~2700–3000K decorative/night. `confirmed`, level 1, single video. [source: yt_iRbTlv5Ka9o]

Now the **highest price tier and broadest category coverage of any source in this store**. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/case_studies/buro_segment_pricing_2026_case|full case study]]. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- Yana Vrublevskaya's Minsk Mir studio renovation (2023, self-managed, real project, ≈$12,957 total against a $10,000 target) — a coherent, arithmetic-checkable, real self-managed project covering both rough works and full finish/furnishing. **Built as a scoped case study directly**, given its evident coherence and its unusually strong structural match to this project's own self-managed plan. See [[11_Budget_and_Planning/_supporting/case_studies/yana_vrublevskaya_minsk_mir_studio_2023_case|full case study]]. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- 46 m² apartment room-by-room electrical point walkthrough (bedside two-way switches, TV-glare-avoidance light placement, vanity/wardrobe/desk outlet counts, centralized hallway vacuum outlets, kitchen electrics deliberately deferred to final cabinetry). No pricing content — a layout-pattern reference, not a cost benchmark. [source: yt_TM-z3NUojII]
- ~100 m² apartment full electrical layout walkthrough (two-way bedside switch + 2 outlets per side of bed; cross-room switch pattern controlling the next room's light before entering it; AC placed behind/above the headboard rather than directly over the bed). [source: yt_iBPJimtw7k0]

## Rules / Heuristics

*The wiki pages already contain synthesized heuristics (buffer %, rough/finish material split, labor-vs-total ratio) traced to the Minsk case studies above. Newly added from this pass:*

- Before setting a renovation budget tier, confirm which portion of the client's stated total is guaranteed cash on hand vs. loans/credit/future income. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- When comparing built-in vs. surface-mounted fixture options, price the full installation (pipework, fittings, labor), not just the device sticker price. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- Vet a contractor via genuine (non-arranged) reviews, an in-person visit to a real completed past project, and review of the actual signed contract. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- A complete design project package should include dimensioned as-built/demolition/clean-montage plans, cross-checked elevations, visualizations, and node/section drawings; MEP engineering is typically out of scope by default. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- Hire independent technical supervision (not affiliated with the renovation contractor) to sign off on each construction stage before the next stage builds over it. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- A single multi-trade "brigade" contracting model is often cheaper than hiring per-trade specialists individually, for a comparable quality outcome most owners won't perceive the difference on. `unverified`. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- Where hardware quality genuinely affects longevity (e.g. drawer slides/hinges), paying a premium for a known-durable brand can be worth it even under an otherwise "don't overpay for brand" approach — treat this as an explicit, narrow exception, not a blanket rule. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- For entryway flooring specifically, prefer a hard, scratch-resistant surface (e.g. porcelain tile) over a wood-look surface, even where wood-look flooring is otherwise the preferred choice elsewhere in the home — tracked-in grit is the reason given. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- Before finalizing a room-count layout, check window count first (a balcony door counts as a quasi-window) — more rooms than usable light sources tends to produce impractically small, poorly-lit rooms. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- When relocating a sink, place it along a wall (not mid-room) so the gravity-fed drain line can run inside the wall to the riser without losing the required slope. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- Interior door rough openings should be sized ~10 cm wider than the leaf (5 cm clearance per side); check door-swing paths for furniture conflicts before defaulting to an 80 cm leaf everywhere. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- A wet zone (kitchen or bathroom) generally must stay stacked above the same wet zone in the apartment below; even a free-planning/flexible-layout unit still needs formal approval to deviate from this. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- Avoid a bathroom entrance directly off a kitchen/dining area; keep the bathroom door at least ~1–1.5 m from the entryway to avoid frequent crossing of the "dirty" entry zone. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- Never touch, damage, or attempt to remove a ventilation shaft ("venshakhta," identifiable by grille openings, typically ~60×40 cm) — it serves every apartment on that riser column, not just the unit being renovated. A plumbing cladding box ("zashivka") is a different structure and can often be rebuilt more compactly. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- Default to leaving developer-installed radiators in place — relocating one requires its own approval project and an energy-efficiency recalculation, a real bureaucratic cost most projects can avoid. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- Engage a specialist for replanning approval from the start rather than attempting to self-manage the process to save money — a documented attempt to DIY it cost one project a full month with nothing to show for it. `single-account`. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- A dedicated laundry/utility room, where floor plan allows, is a comparatively low-cost, high comfort-value addition; where space doesn't allow it, a combo washer-dryer unit is a reasonable substitute for a separate stacked dryer. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- Rough AC sizing rule of thumb: ~1 kW cooling capacity per 10 m² of room area, minimum practical unit size 2 kW, with a ~20% capacity buffer as standard practice — adjust upward for sun exposure or rooms that share airflow with an open door. `confirmed` as the source's own stated rule of thumb, explicitly flagged by the source itself as approximate. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- Buy AC equipment and installation from the same company where possible, to get a single bundled warranty rather than having to attribute fault between separate equipment and installation vendors after a failure. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- Photograph in-wall drain/refrigerant line routing during installation, to reduce the risk of later wall work (e.g. hanging shelves, drilling) damaging a hidden line. [source: yt_H61xa8n2nTk]
- Price water/heating rough-in by fixture "points" (e.g. a sink needs 2: hot + cold), not by linear meters of pipe — pricing by pipe length is a common vector for "turns out we needed more, pay more" upselling. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- Price electrical rough-in by floor area, not by cable length, for the same anti-upsell reason. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- Verify a smeta specifies plaster/putty quality grade and flooring installation pattern (straight/diagonal/herringbone) explicitly — both materially affect cost and are common sources of "we assumed the cheaper option, pay extra now" disputes if left unspecified. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- Watch for scope commonly added mid-project rather than itemized upfront (partition-wall reinforcement/opening framing, plaster corner-bead/window-reveal work, ancillary tile-cutting work) and common-area/lobby protective-covering requirements some residential complexes impose — verify these appear as explicit line items in the original smeta. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- Match renovation ambition to the property's own class — don't commission a premium designer renovation in a low-value unit, and don't do bare cosmetic work in an expensive new-build. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- If budget-constrained, prefer buying an already-finished (even unremarkable) developer unit over a bare-shell unit you might not have enough funds to fully renovate — running out of money mid-project (e.g. stalling at tiling) produces a worse outcome. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- If self-managing on a tight budget, it's reasonable to reduce the plumbing equipment set itself (fewer valves/reducers = fewer failure points), as distinct from just downgrading brand within a fixed set. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- Don't save incrementally toward a "someday" quality renovation — renovation costs (materials, furniture, labor) tend to inflate faster than typical personal savings accumulate; commit to a simplified scope within current means now, or defer the decision entirely. `single-account`, opinion. [source: yt_OrKB6uSKRyk]
- Never quote or compare a renovation by "price per m²" alone — always ask what specific material/build-quality/complexity level that figure assumes, since the same visual outcome can vary 2–10× in real cost. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- When evaluating a material for a given application, separate "will it survive functionally" (durability) from "will it look/install correctly" (quality) — a budget material can be equally durable while being harder or impossible to install well due to poor geometry/consistency. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- Don't prioritize spending on labor/execution quality over materials, and don't cut cost on rough materials or concealed systems (heating, plumbing, junctions) — poor execution undermines even good materials, and concealed-system replacement later is expensive or impossible. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- When budgeting, separately account for "wishes" (a specific named product/brand target) and "expectations" (a described functional requirement) as their own cost drivers — both can swing a single line item's cost by up to 10×, independent of overall quality tier. [source: yt_6Z7uH2_rXsw]
- Prefer a local wall-mounted "breather" ventilation unit over a full mechanical supply-and-exhaust system for most apartments — breathers are claimed to solve the large majority of comfort/air-quality needs at a fraction of the cost; reserve a full system for apartments on loud arterial roads where windows can't be opened for ventilation. [source: yt_DsdLa87Acz4]
- A specific, checkable upsell red flag: a company that deliberately underbids an initial quote by ~10–15%, then adds roughly 40% more once work is underway — treat an unusually cheap initial quote from a company with skepticism rather than as a win. [source: yt_cdNwbqsLUK4]
- For a child's room specifically, place the bed away from the window/heat source and the desk closer to the natural light source. [source: yt_QHl1YEHMfgE]
- When budgeting a multi-month self-managed project in a currency loosely pegged to USD, confirm and track exchange-rate drift explicitly rather than assuming a fixed rate — a real project's own rate moved from 2.6 to 2.8 BYN/USD over its duration, meaningfully affecting the USD-equivalent total even with local-currency costs unchanged. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- Budget contingency isn't only relevant to turnkey/company-managed projects — even a disciplined, itemized self-managed renovation can meaningfully overshoot its own target (here, ~30%); treat a stated self-managed target figure as a floor to plan around, not a ceiling to expect. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- When switching a major appliance's install method late (e.g. freestanding to built-in) after rough electrical is already done, budget for a same-day emergency callout to relocate the affected outlet — a small, concrete cost of late decisions. [source: yt_8zm_5NjbGNA]
- Default interior door leaf widths: 80 cm for living spaces (70 cm only if 80 doesn't physically fit; never 90 cm), 70 cm for wet rooms/utility rooms as the speaker's revised preference (60 cm only if space is very tight). Size the rough opening 10 cm wider than the leaf, and never let the finished casing sit flush against an adjacent wall/corner. [source: yt_ludvy76HGSU]
- Always demolish and rebuild standard brick boxing ("короб") around a plumbing/heating riser rather than leaving it in place, for three reasons: it hides leaks/defects until they surface after finishes are complete (by which point only mitigation, not repair, is possible); it blocks soundproofing the riser itself; and it typically costs ~15 cm of usable floor area versus building the finish wall directly at the riser's own inspection-valve plane. `single-account`, sourced from a commercial (dental clinic) project — the demolish-and-inspect rationale generalizes, but the specific corridor-width code figure cited alongside it (120 cm minimum) is a medical-facility standard, not confirmed residential. [source: yt_PO9kd1cqCyA]
- Avoid sliding interior doors by default (soundproofing, reliability, and repairability are all materially worse than hinged doors); reserve them for a narrow exception — a private ensuite serving only 1-2 people where a hinged door's swing has no viable path — and even then expect roller/stopper/damper mechanism failures within a few years. [source: yt_1YiVgB9jqyU; yt_x4TlTKt211Q]
- When forming any casing-trimmed opening (with or without a door leaf) between two rooms, preserve a small wall stub (~10 cm) at each outer corner so casing can terminate cleanly on both faces — don't let a site crew remove it without checking against the original design/technologist plan. [source: yt_c4b7iyg8v5U]
- Treat a door's own casing-standoff/junction-detail conventions as defaults, not absolutes — when a hard physical constraint (a neighboring wall, an embedded utility box) forces a trade-off, prioritize a door's functional clear width (never go below roughly 74 cm for an entrance door) over the cosmetic standoff rule. [source: yt_D2Qz6s8eNoU]
- Set interior door swing direction by room type: living spaces (bedroom, living room, study, kids' room, kitchen) open inward; WCs/bathrooms/small utility rooms open outward (partly for space, and critically so an incapacitated occupant blocking the door from inside can still be reached). Quick check: a room whose light switch is inside gets an inward-opening door; a room whose switch is outside gets an outward-opening door. [source: yt_7Vd95idVXak]

### Electrical / Lighting (new topic area, added 2026-08-04)

- Set switch heights (90 cm from floor, 15 cm from door edge) and bedside/countertop outlet heights (70 cm / 110 cm) as fixed defaults across an entire apartment, not per-occupant — they're ergonomic constants (hand-drop height, furniture standards), not something that scales with who lives there. [source: yt_uLJGsTfTj3A, yt_l_rjjPlPkRo]
- Size and select circuit breakers by pairing curve type to load (curve B sockets, curve C lighting/AC) and amperage to cable gauge, not by amperage alone — an oversized breaker on undersized cable defeats the breaker's protective purpose. [source: yt_JrClDJb8WTM, yt_ciXeqvKDKSI]
- Count and buy finish-stage electrical devices room-by-room from the as-built rough-in, roughly a week before install, not from the original drawing months in advance — kitchen point counts in particular commonly shift once final cabinetry is confirmed. [source: yt_PIYzs2b4UDU]
- When vetting an electrical contractor, check for a dust-extraction vacuum on their tools and inspect drilled socket-box holes for clean, round cuts — both are checkable-by-a-non-expert signals of installation quality. [source: yt_XmeoZUiGwBM]
- Plan electrical layout against a 5-15 year family-change horizon (not just move-in day), and leave a few extra outlets wired-but-hidden behind the finished wall surface as a low-cost future-proofing move. [source: yt_k7VUlw8gHZs]

### Design-Project Process & Deliverable Structure (new topic area, added 2026-08-10)

- Use a design-project deliverable checklist (see this store's 28-item list) to audit whether a self-managed plan's own documentation has real gaps, regardless of whether a company like this one is actually engaged. [source: web_zemspro_about_development]
- Bound mid-process change requests to an explicit, narrow window (e.g. "one more outlet, not a redesign") rather than leaving revision scope open-ended at a review checkpoint. [source: web_zemspro_about_development]
- Route quality-control review through more than one role/person per major stage (not just the person who did the work) before a deliverable reaches the client. [source: web_zemspro_about_development]

### Furniture / Built-ins: Wardrobe & Closet Design (new topic area, added 2026-08-10)

- Default to a plain wardrobe over a single-sided walk-in closet unless the room is genuinely large enough (≥5m long at typical width) to lose nothing else by adding one — two back-to-back wardrobes sharing a thin dividing wall between adjoining rooms is usually a better use of the same footprint. [source: yt_RyHzFDGgqKA, yt_Xh7uwbKVmfA]
- When sizing a built-in sliding-door wardrobe's depth, don't stop at the hanging-garment depth alone — add a 2-3cm/side air gap and the sliding-track allowance (~10cm), or the wardrobe will end up materially shallower than planned. [source: yt_VgsPDOcPV7c]
- Lock in bed and TV placement before wardrobe placement in a tight bedroom — if no wall remains for storage afterward, consider reconfiguring the window/balcony opening itself rather than accepting no wardrobe. [source: yt_CHCB4KPupyc]
- Before ordering glass wardrobe-door panels, scratch-test the actual glass with a fingernail, and never skip soft-close dampers on heavy glass doors. [source: yt_8odGOTVAuIk]
- Never place mirrored wardrobe panels where they'd reflect peripheral-vision motion from the bed, and never rely on recessed spotlights inside an enclosed mirrored closet — use a luminous ceiling panel instead. [source: yt_8odGOTVAuIk]
- In a dispute with a furniture/renovation contractor, determine early whether the work is built-in (affects interior finishing, unlocks a broader restoration-cost claim) or free-standing (goods-only claim) — this determines which type of formal expert examination applies and what can be claimed. [source: yt_pVMvsuhrwWs]

### Interior Decor / Layout Planning (new topic area, added 2026-08-05)

- Before installing slat-wall backlighting, set the slat spacing to match the light-channel box's own module — decide this at design time, not after the slats are up. [source: yt_1OLVQUJkOdM]
- Always level a wall before installing wall panels, regardless of the panel finish planned over it. [source: yt_1OLVQUJkOdM]
- Default to smooth, solid wall panels over milled/routed ones unless the client accepts the added cleaning burden of the recesses. [source: yt_1OLVQUJkOdM]
- Never specify velour for a wall, ceiling, or floor surface. [source: yt_1OLVQUJkOdM]
- When floating furniture is planned, specify the required wall prep and load-rated mounting hardware explicitly in the design documentation — don't leave it implicit even on an already-dense wall. [source: yt_1OLVQUJkOdM]
- Decide kitchen/living-room combination by the specific household's guest frequency and overnight-guest duration, not as a default layout choice. [source: yt_cp8U9wvsqI8]
- Verify windowsill height on-site before designing a windowsill desk feature, and always pair such a feature with blackout/opaque blinds. [source: yt_cp8U9wvsqI8]
- For a room expected to be reconfigured later (e.g. a child's room ahead of a sibling), pre-install concealed dual-position electrical rough-ins at build time rather than requiring a rewire later. [source: yt_cp8U9wvsqI8]
- Plan washing-machine placement for access without entering the bathroom/WC when a household's laundry frequency is expected to be high. [source: yt_cp8U9wvsqI8]
- Decide shower-vs-tub and similar fixture trade-offs via a direct conversation with the primary occupants about their actual preference, rather than defaulting to a layout that also accommodates infrequent guests. [source: yt_cp8U9wvsqI8]
- Size and split closet/storage allocation by actual use case (guest vs. resident, by activity/gear type, or a dedicated low-visibility storage zone) rather than defaulting to one generic closet. [source: yt_cp8U9wvsqI8]

### Second Turboscribe Batch (added 2026-08-10)

- Never make a bathtub siphon revision hatch quick-release/magnetic — tile and silicone-seal it, and store spare tile/grout/an unopened silicone tube inside that same sealed space. [source: yt_3EBbFbhdj6g]
- When sizing a bathtub, fit its length to the actual occupant's height rather than a generic default, and always center the mixer on the tub's true geometry, not the tile-overhung visible portion. [source: yt_SBzDJk_yp8w]
- Never deviate from an already-finalized design during construction to save a small amount — the later retrofit cost is commonly several times the original saving. [source: yt_u5wcPgJPDrs]
- Proactively suggest a low-mounted "bottom tap" to clients with pets, foot-washing habits, or reduced mobility — most won't know to ask for it. [source: yt_u5wcPgJPDrs]
- Route flooring-material transitions through a door opening (terminating at the frame) rather than across open floor, and match plank-grain direction to each zone's own dominant traffic direction. [source: yt_UfmUC4-T3jY]
- Split an L-shaped room/zone with a small dividing wall before finalizing wardrobe, ceiling, or flooring plans for it — the cost of the wall is often far smaller than the combined savings across all three trades. [source: yt_UfmUC4-T3jY]
- When choosing interior doors, decide style first, then match casing/baseboard/hardware to it, and only then select the entrance door's interior trim panel to match — not the reverse. [source: yt_ti8J19zY0EM]
- Avoid active deadbolt pins on an entrance door; rely on passive pins plus a proper two-lock (armored + re-pinnable) setup instead. [source: yt_ti8J19zY0EM]
- Do not commit to both a cork floor and a large dog in the same household — verify this kind of material/lifestyle conflict before finalizing material selection. [source: yt_z3xJeVPL6n4]
- Treat furniture layout, corrected room layout, and client lifestyle/physical factors as strict prerequisites for electrical design — never let a client commission "just the electrical" or "just the layout" in isolation. [source: yt_z3xJeVPL6n4]

### Kitchen / Kitchen Appliances (new topic area, added 2026-08-14)

- Before finalizing a kitchen niche/recess, verify in sequence that it fits the sink, cooktop, main prep zone, a built-in column-stacked refrigerator, and an oven+microwave column — check the full appliance set, not just the run length. [source: yt_gvNczaG3SLY]
- Decide a kitchen/living-room door's swing direction from its actual expected usage pattern: swing toward the seating area if the door will usually stay closed (so it self-closes toward habit); swing the other way (dropping a now-redundant decorative element) if it will usually stay open; skip the door entirely for a decorative portal if a door isn't functionally needed. [source: yt_gvNczaG3SLY]
- When choosing a food-waste disposer, treat fibrous produce (peels, trimmings) as the real hazard to plan usage habits around, not hard items like bones — brief the household accordingly rather than assuming intuition will get it right. [source: yt_9vcDyi4utLk]
- When speccing a built-in refrigerator with a touch-open facade, always pair it with a milled pull handle on adjacent cabinetry — fingerprints on the fridge face are unavoidable regardless of finish, but adjacent-cabinet fingerprints are avoidable. [source: yt_7VfZIYGUrTo]
- Never repaint a kitchen-module interior panel to match a custom facade color after fabrication — order it pre-colored on all sides from the factory instead. [source: yt_7VfZIYGUrTo]
- When a building shell has irregular (trapezoidal/curved) geometry, absorb the irregularity into circulation space (a corridor) rather than into a wet room — a wet room should always come out perfectly rectangular regardless of the shell it's built into. [source: yt_RF6FdHk_jgE]
- When sizing a false-wall utility-concealment box, design for the full hardware assembly (manifold, panel) it must contain, not the minimum clearance needed to hide the pipework alone. [source: yt_RF6FdHk_jgE]
- Before dismissing a developer-provided storage niche as usable based on its stated footprint, check what's physically behind that wall — hidden service space or a bad open external corner can make an on-paper-correct niche functionally unusable. [source: yt_UoqC7xSFZ4Q]
- When evaluating whether a small wet/utility room can fit a given set of fixtures, check the doorway's position first — it's often the real binding constraint, not the room's raw floor area. [source: yt_UoqC7xSFZ4Q]
- Before keeping a loggia's original exterior insulation after adding warm/insulated glazing, evaluate whether it's now redundant — removing a genuinely redundant insulation layer can reclaim meaningful interior width (a real example gained ~19-20 cm). [source: yt_UoqC7xSFZ4Q]
- Never place a study/desk position facing a window without pairing it with a full light-blocking blind/film treatment — treat this as mandatory whenever a window-facing desk placement is chosen, not an optional finishing touch. [source: yt_UoqC7xSFZ4Q]

### Design Concept (new topic area, added 2026-08-15)

- **A materials/color palette can be deliberately designed for cross-scale reuse rather than bespoke-fitted per apartment** — choosing finishes/colors specifically because they harmonize with each other regardless of room proportions lets one design concept transfer to a different footprint without a full redesign. `single-account`, presented as this company's own product mechanism, but the underlying principle (palette chosen for cross-scale harmony, not just per-project fit) is a genuinely reusable design idea. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Layer lighting into three distinct types — primary/functional, decorative, and duty/night — rather than one fixed setup**, so different lighting "scenarios" (e.g. dim diffused light for watching a film vs. bright light for a gathering) can be built from the same fixtures without rewiring. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Slat/batten wall cladding and mirrors are both low-cost techniques for making a renovation look more expensive than its actual cost** — slats specifically called out as "one of the most effective, accessible" modern-style techniques at relatively low price; mirrors double as a low-cost space-expansion technique and are sometimes combined with slats in the same room. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Match floor and furniture textures deliberately** (choose flooring's natural-look texture first, then select furniture textures that closely match it) as a cohesion technique for a "loaded but not cluttered" style. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Use a track/magnetic-rail lighting system for primary lighting** to keep fixture position, count, and type changeable after installation, without rewiring — a flexibility-over-time technique, not a one-time install decision. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **A design-fee-worthiness threshold should be checked against project scale, not treated as a flat always/never rule** — the speaker's framing (bespoke design "pays for itself" only above a stated renovation-budget threshold) is a reusable evaluation *shape* even though the specific threshold (30M RUB) is this one company's own self-reported, Moscow/RUB/2023-specific figure — do not reuse the number itself without re-deriving it for a different market/year. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Keep detail count deliberately low (a small number of repeated textures/colors/details) to avoid a "loaded" modern-style look, while still avoiding classic minimalism's cold/empty feel** — the stated middle-ground goal between two failure modes, not just "less is more." [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Use consistent porcelain tile without pattern inserts throughout wet rooms, and vary visual interest through colored furniture/backlighting instead of tile-pattern changes** — a technique for keeping a material palette minimal while still avoiding a flat/monotonous wet room. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Add a profile LED strip with a diffuser alongside slat cladding** to gain a second lighting scenario option per room "at practically no cost increase" — a low-marginal-cost way to extend the lighting-layering technique above into rooms that already have slats installed. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]
- **Design custom kids'-room furniture to scale to the room's actual footprint while preserving consistent seating usability for schoolwork**, rather than a fixed-dimension piece resized proportionally — keeps the furniture functional regardless of the specific apartment's kids-room size. [source: yt_OCjVd5HOcac]

## Decisions

None yet.

## Assumptions

- Price figures from `yt_BMmPLHVmnqw` beyond the confirmed 100–120 тыс./м² headline figure (i.e. the 35-45, 220, and 20/30/40/60/80 numbers inside the ASR transcript body) are *assumed* to follow the same "тыс. руб." convention established by the confirmed headline figure, since the transcript drops the unit after first mention (common in spoken Russian) — this is a consistency-based inference (level 3), not an independent confirmation of each individual number. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]
- ~~All price figures from `yt_BMmPLHVmnqw` are assumed RUB...~~ **Partially resolved 2026-07-30**: currency (RUB) and publish date (2025-04-29) are `confirmed` via YouTube's own video metadata (level 2 — title/description/upload_date fields, independent of the ASR transcript). **Region is explicitly NOT collapsed into "confirmed Moscow"** — see the dedicated Regional Applicability entry in Risks/Gaps below; the uploader-location field is metadata about the channel, not a confirmed claim about the market the discussed prices apply to. Still treated as secondary reference only, not Minsk/Belarus-equivalent, per the guide's existing region caveat. [source: yt_BMmPLHVmnqw]

## Risks / Gaps

- No source yet establishes a min/max price distribution across projects — the case study for this is planned but not created (see [[11_Budget_and_Planning/Budgeting_Guide|Budgeting Guide]] §6).
- `price_table_screenshot_case.md` mixes budgeting evidence with unrelated screenshot/parser test-fixture content; not yet split.

- 2026-08-15 — **Batch-level assessment, title-skim selection of 4 Zemstandart/Zemskov videos for positive "how to gain/optimize space" framing vs. the channel's usual dunk-format critiques**: mixed result, not a uniform win. Two videos (`mdDZOneVlzU`, `fnTjaXPEDDE`) were genuinely the densest, least-promotional sources processed from this channel to date — supporting the title-skim hypothesis. One video (`yt_E6oQ8XQ765M`) was moderately useful but carried substantially more direct sales pitching than its batch companions. One video (`yt_Z-3sMXvBHK8`) had low marginal value — near-duplicate script/pricing campaign to its closest batch companion and likely the same underlying project as an already-processed source. **Refined takeaway for future title-skim batches**: a positive "how to" title framing does correlate with denser technique content, but does not reliably predict it on its own — this channel reuses scripted segments (price pitches, process narration) and possibly project material across similarly-titled videos in the same short time window, so a title-skim selection pass should also flag videos uploaded close in time to each other or to an already-processed video's upload date, and cross-check for apparent project/script overlap, not rely on title sentiment alone.
